Elastic nuclear recoil (\cref{lim:1.3a}), \cref{eq:1.3-ERmax,eq:1.3-vmin}
& \cite[Eqs.~(77), (78)]{lin2019tasi}
& \Cref{eq:1.3-omegaq} with $\Delta_\chi = 0$ and a free nucleus at rest, $\omega = q^2/2M$, reduces to \cref{eq:1.3-elastic}; maximizing over the angle gives $q \le 2\mu_{\chi N}v$ and \cref{eq:1.3-ERmax}. \Cref{eq:1.3-vmin} at $\omega = E_R = q^2/2M$ gives $\vmin = q/2\mu_{\chi N}$.
& Impulse approximation: the nucleus recoils as if free and at rest. In the harmonic model with $\omega_0 = \SI{60}{meV}$ the rms width of the deposit is below $E_R/3$ for $E_R \ge 9\omega_0 = \SI{0.54}{eV}$ in silicon. Transfer below the inverse nuclear radius, so the nucleus recoils as a whole.
& \Fsize{relative width $(\omega_0/E_R)^{1/2} \le 1/3$ for $E_R \ge \SI{0.54}{eV}$ (Si)}. Binding to the lattice replaces $\delta(\omega - q^2/2M)$ by a peak of mean $E_R$ and rms width $(E_R\omega_0)^{1/2}$~\cite[Sect.~IV\,A, Eqs.~(90), (91)]{kahn2022searches}; for $E_R \lesssim 3\omega_0$ it breaks into single- and few-phonon lines (\cref{chap:5}).
& \cref{sec:1.3} \\
\midrule
Heavy and light mediators, $\FDM = 1$ and $(q_0/q)^2$ (\cref{lim:1.4a}), \cref{eq:1.4-yukawa-sigma}
& \cite[Eq.~(15) and the display above it]{essig2012direct}
& \Cref{eq:1.4-yukawa-sigma} with $m_V \gg q, q_0$ for every contributing $q$ gives $\FDM = 1$ and $\sigmae = \mu_{\chi e}^2y_\chi^2y_e^2/(\pi m_V^4)$; with $m_V \ll q, q_0$ it gives $\FDM = (q_0/q)^2$ and $\sigmae = \mu_{\chi e}^2y_\chi^2y_e^2/(\pi q_0^4)$.
& Heavy: $m_V$ above the largest transfer, $2m_\chi v_{\mathrm{max}}$ (\SI{0.5}{MeV} at $m_\chi = \SI{100}{MeV}$); within \SI{10}{\percent} for $0.11\,q_0 \le q \le q_0$ when $m_V \ge 4.5\,q_0$. Light: $m_V$ below the smallest transfer, $\omega/v_{\mathrm{max}} \approx 0.11\,q_0$ for the \SI{1.11}{eV} gap; within \SI{10}{\percent} for all $q \ge 0.11\,q_0$ when $m_V \le 0.026\,q_0$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{10}{\percent} for $m_V \ge 4.5\,q_0$ (heavy) or $m_V \le 0.026\,q_0$ (light)}. Heavy, $\FDM \simeq 1 + (q_0^2 - q^2)/m_V^2$; light, $\FDM \simeq (q_0/q)^2(1 + m_V^2/q_0^2 - m_V^2/q^2)$.
& \cref{sec:1.4} \\
\midrule
Atomic ionization rate (\cref{lim:2.2a}), \cref{eq:2.2-ion,eq:2.2-fion}
& \cite[Eqs.~(5), (6); Eq.~(1) for the Fermi factor]{essig2012direct}
& \Cref{eq:2.2-spectrum} summed over the occupied states of the shell; outgoing waves normalized to $(2\pi)^3\delta_{l'l}\delta_{m'm}\delta(p' - p)/p^2$, so $\sum_f = \sum_{l'm'}\int p_{\mathrm{ej}}^2\dd p_{\mathrm{ej}}/(2\pi)^3$ and $p_{\mathrm{ej}}^2\dd p_{\mathrm{ej}} = (p_{\mathrm{ej}}^3/2)\dd\ln E_{\mathrm{ej}}$; with $|\tilde V|^2 = \pi\sigmae\FDM^2/\mu_{\chi e}^2$ the prefactor is $\sigmae/(8\mu_{\chi e}^2)$.
& First Born approximation in the dark-matter potential; one electron at a time in a fixed atomic potential (no correlation, no recoil of the ion).
& \Fsize{about \SI{30}{\percent} (outer shells of heavy atoms); $\times0.46$ (H 1s, \SI{1}{GeV})}. Distortion of the outgoing wave by the ion, growing as $E_{\mathrm{ej}}$ falls. Plane wave times the Fermi factor with $Z_{\mathrm{eff}} = 1$: within about \SI{30}{\percent} of exact continuum states for outer shells of heavy atoms~\cite[text after Eq.~(6)]{essig2012direct}; for hydrogen 1s, 0.46 of the exact rate at $m_\chi = \SI{1}{GeV}$ (\cref{fig:2-ionization-limit}).
& \cref{sec:2.2} \\

Thomas--Fermi screening (\cref{lim:4.5a}), \cref{eq:4.4-TF}
& \cite[Eq.~(S.14)]{hochberg2021determining}
& Random-phase approximation \cref{eq:4.5-rpa} with the static Lindhard function \cref{eq:4.5-static}, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}}(q,0) = 1 + (k_{\mathrm{TF}}^2/q^2)F_{\mathrm{L}}(q/2k_{\mathrm{F}})$; $F_{\mathrm{L}} \to 1$ as $q \to 0$; $k_{\mathrm{TF}}^2 = 2e^2\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{F}}$ (\cref{eq:4.4-TF}).
& Static potential in a homogeneous electron gas, $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}$: within 2 percent of the Lindhard value for $q < 0.49\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$, 10 percent at $q = 1.06\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$ (\cref{fig:4-tf-limit}). Not for an insulator at $q$ below about $k_{\mathrm{TF}}/(\varepsilon_\infty - 1)^{1/2}$ (\cref{we:4-tf}).
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{2}{\percent} for $q < 0.49\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$; \SI{10}{\percent} at $1.06\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$}. $F_{\mathrm{L}} = 1 - q^2/12k_{\mathrm{F}}^2 + O(q^4)$ (\cref{eq:C.8-FL}); exchange--correlation corrections to the proper response (\cref{sec:4.6}).
& \cref{sec:4.5} \\
\midrule
Plasmon dispersion and cutoff (\cref{lim:4.5b}), \cref{eq:4.5-dispersion}
& \cite[Eq.~(171) and the text after it]{kahn2022searches}
& Zero of $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}} = 1 - v_{\mathrm{C}}R^0$ above the continuum; $R^0$ expanded in $1/z^2$ through the odd moments of the pair energies over the Fermi sphere (\cref{eq:4.5-moments}); iteration of $\omega^2 = \omega_p^2(1 + A/\omega^2 + B/\omega^4)$; cutoff where $\omega_{\mathrm{pl}}$ meets $\omega_+ = qv_{\mathrm{F}} + q^2/2m_e$, estimated with $\omega_+ \simeq qv_{\mathrm{F}}$.
& Random-phase approximation, $T = 0$, no collisions; $q \ll q_{\mathrm{c}}$. At $r_s = 2$ the quadratic formula is within 1 percent of the exact root for $q < 0.39\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$; exact $q_{\mathrm{c}} = 0.730\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$ against the estimate $0.666\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$ (\cref{fig:4-plasmon-limit}).
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{1}{\percent} for $q < 0.39\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$ ($r_s = 2$); cutoff estimate \SI{9}{\percent} low}. $\bigl[1/4m_e^2 + (12/175)v_{\mathrm{F}}^4/\omega_p^2\bigr]q^4$ in $\omega_{\mathrm{pl}}^2$ (\cref{eq:4.5-dispersion}); local-field (exchange--correlation) corrections (\cref{sec:4.6}); damping by collisions (\cref{eq:4.5-mermin}) or interband decay (\cref{we:4-siplasmon}).
& \cref{sec:4.5} \\

Gapless Dirac dielectric function (\cref{lim:7.3a}), \cref{eq:D.22-gapless}
& \cite[Eq.~(2.7)]{hochberg2018detection}, whose cutoff is a hard cutoff; $g_{\mathrm{deg}}$ taken out of the coupling
& Interband RPA bubble of one isotropic cone at $T = 0$, chemical potential in the gap, hard cutoff $|\vect{k}| < \Lambda_{\mathrm{D}}$ (\cref{eq:D.22-R}); background added as $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}$ (\cref{eq:7.3-epsbg}); gapped result \cref{eq:D.22-eps}; logarithm expanded for $|\omega^2 - v_{\mathrm{D}}^2q^2| \gg \Delta_{\mathrm{D}}^2$ with $\int_0^1x(1 - x)\ln[x(1 - x)]\,\dd x = -5/18$ (\cref{eq:D.22-gapless})
& $|v_{\mathrm{D}}^2q^2 - \omega^2| \gg 4\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}^2$; $q, \omega/v_{\mathrm{D}} \ll \Lambda_{\mathrm{D}}$; $g_{\mathrm{deg}}\tilde\alpha\ln(\Lambda_{\mathrm{D}}/q) \lesssim 1$. Static: within 0.1 (in units of $g_{\mathrm{deg}}\tilde\alpha/6\pi$) of the gapped result for $v_{\mathrm{D}}q > 7.5\,\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}$ (\cref{fig:7-gapless-limit})
& \Fsize{$\le 0.1$ (units of $g_{\mathrm{deg}}\tilde\alpha/6\pi$) for $v_{\mathrm{D}}q > 7.5\,\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}$}. $-6\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}^2/(v_{\mathrm{D}}^2q^2 - \omega^2)$ in the bracket (residual $3\times10^{-3}$ at $v_{\mathrm{D}}q = 10\,\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}$); anisotropy by rescaling (\cref{eq:7.3-aniso}); higher orders in $\tilde\alpha$
& \cref{sec:7.3} \\

Second Born correction for a Yukawa potential (\cref{lim:8.2a}), \cref{eq:8.2-born}
& \cite[Chap.~6]{sakurai2017modern} (conditions $|f|m_V \ll 1$ and $y_\chi y_T/$ $4\pi v \ll 1$, \cref{sec:2.2})
& Born series of \cref{eq:2.2-born} to second order in $\tilde V$; zero-energy radial equation, $a = a_{\mathrm{Born}}(1 \pm |f(0)|m_V/2)$, $\sigma = 4\pi a^2$; partial waves by the variable-phase method at $|f(0)|m_V = 10^{-3}$, $p_{\mathrm{rel}}/m_V = 0.1$--10; coupling from $\sigmae$ or $\sigma_p$ with $\FDM$ normalized at $q_0$ (\cref{eq:1.4-yukawa-sigma}); $p_{\mathrm{rel}} = \mu_{\chi T}v$, $v = 10^{-3}$.
& Exact coefficient for $p_{\mathrm{rel}} \ll m_V$; within \SI{30}{\percent} of the largest partial-wave correction (total and $\dd\sigma/\dd\Omega$, $q \le 2p_{\mathrm{rel}}$) for $p_{\mathrm{rel}} \le m_V$, above it for $p_{\mathrm{rel}} \ge 3m_V$ (0.15 of it at $10\,m_V$). Plot (\cref{fig:8-born-limit}): $x = \mchi$, 1\,keV--1\,GeV; $y = m_V$, 1\,eV--10\,GeV; contours of the cross-section at $|\delta_{\mathrm{Born}}| = 0.1$, electrons and silicon nuclei; gray where that needs couplings above $4\pi$. At $\sigmae = \SI{e-37}{cm^2}$, $\sigma_p = \SI{e-40}{cm^2}$: $|\delta_{\mathrm{Born}}| \le 4.5\times10^{-5}$, $8.0\times10^{-5}$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{30}{\percent} of $\delta_{\mathrm{Born}}$ for $p_{\mathrm{rel}} \le m_V$; $|\delta_{\mathrm{Born}}| \le 8.0\times10^{-5}$ at the benchmark cross-sections}. Order $(y_\chi y_T)^2$ relative: resonances of the attractive potential for $|f(0)|m_V \gtrsim 1$; for $p_{\mathrm{rel}} \gg m_V$ the correction falls below $y_\chi y_T/4\pi v$ (0.28 of it at $q = 2p_{\mathrm{rel}} = 20\,m_V$); the speed of a bound electron raises $p_{\mathrm{rel}}$ and lowers the correction.
& \cref{sec:8.2} \\

& \Fsize{dropped transverse term $r_{\mathrm{IP}} = 22$--56 times the kept one (Si, 3--\SI{30}{MeV})}. The transverse motion of the electron, $\eta\,\Tr\bar S_{\vect{j}_{\mathrm{T}}\vect{j}_{\mathrm{T}}}$ in \cref{eq:10.4-O8}, larger than the term kept by $r_{\mathrm{IP}} = 22$--56 in silicon between 30 and \SI{3}{MeV}, and the spin-current row $\mathcal{J}_{\mathrm{T}}$ of $\mathcal{O}_3$, $\mathcal{O}_7$, $\mathcal{O}_{12}$--$\mathcal{O}_{15}$ (one quarter of the current block for independent particles); then the cross term, half-width $2\sqrt r$ relative to $1 + r$, zero after direction averaging; then the local-field change of the current row (model $-4$ to $-15$ percent) and the electron--hole attraction in both blocks (\cref{sec:10.5}).
& \cref{sec:10.4} \\

& \Cref{eq:10.2-rate}; $S$ of the singlet excitons \cref{eq:4.9-S} from \cref{eq:4.9-bse} (static screened ladder, \cref{app:D.12}); $\langle\lambda|n_e|0\rangle = \sqrt{g_{\mathrm{deg}}}\rho_{\lambda0}$ (\cref{eq:10.5-formfactor}); line form valid when no line moves $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{mac}}$ near its energy. Near the pair minimum: Wannier lines and Fermi factor (\cref{eq:10.5-lines}), second class \cref{eq:10.5-second}. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ = BSE (static screened interaction, Tamm--Dancoff), $n_B = 0$.

& \Fsize{about \SI{10}{\percent} on $\varepsilon_2$ (coupling block)}. Field: source current $g_{a\gamma\gamma}(\partial_t\phi)\vect{B}_0$, Amp\`ere--Maxwell at $p_\phi \to 0$, dissipated power over the number density. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ exact with measured $\varepsilon$, at the temperature of the data; above $E_{\mathrm{dir}}$ $n_g = 0$; between the indirect and the direct gap the measured $\sigma_1$ is $n_g = 2$, $n_T = 1$, and a detector needs $n_T = 0$ (\cref{sec:13.5}).

Isotropic field formula exact for cubic crystals and on principal axes; sapphire at \SI{45}{\degree}: within \SI{10}{\percent} on \SI{37}{\percent} of 40--\SI{120}{meV}, low by up to 560 at a longitudinal resonance (\cref{eq:10.7-aniso}). & \Fsize{$O(v^2)$: $-1.7\times10^{-6}$ at \SI{10}{eV}; vacuum mixing off by $4\times10^{-4}$ to 20 below \SI{63}{eV}}. First order in $\Pi_{\mathrm{em}}/m_\phi^2$, $1 + 2(1 - \varepsilon_1)$, useful only above about \SI{36}{eV}; $O(v^2)$ polarization terms; temperature transfer of measured $\sigma_1$ near the indirect edge, more than a factor 2 within \SI{20}{meV} (\cref{sec:13.5}); the field's action on the target, $\mu_{\mathrm{B}}B_0 = \SI{0.58}{meV}$ at \SI{10}{T}.

& \Cref{eq:8.1-scalar}; density row of order $p_\phi$; $\tilde R_{\mathcal{T}_en_e} = \vect{p}_\phi\cdot\vect{F} + O(p_\phi^2)$ with $\vect{F} = 0$ for point groups without a polar vector, so the photon-reducible part is $O(p_\phi^2)$; \cref{eq:8.2-abs} with $g^\phi_{\mathcal{T}_e} = g_{\phi ee}/m_e$. Sum rule: local, one-body and regular potentials only. Plot (\cref{fig:10-scalar-limit}): one s electron; $x = \Lambda$, 1--\SI{2000}{hartree}; $y = \sum_{E_f - E_0 < \Lambda}(E_f - E_0)|\langle f|T|0\rangle|^2$ for $-1/(r^2 + r_{\mathrm{c}}^2)^{1/2}$, $r_{\mathrm{c}} = 0.3$ and $0.1\,a_0$ (\SI{90}{\percent} of the sum rule above 25 and \SI{176}{hartree}), and for $-1/r$, which grows as $(4/\pi)\sqrt{2\Lambda}$ (0.58, 0.74, 0.83 of it at 100, 500, \SI{2000}{hartree}).
& \Fsize{density row $3\times10^{-3}$ (\SI{5}{eV}) to about 1 (16--\SI{18}{eV}) of the kinetic rate}. Sum rule: nonlocal pseudopotentials, $-\langle[[\mathcal{T}_e,V_{\mathrm{NL}}],V_{\mathrm{NL}}]\rangle$ (cf.\ \cref{eq:4.3-fsumNL}); electron--electron forces; divergence for the Coulomb potential of a nucleus (s character at the nucleus).

& \Fsize{about \SI{7.8}{\percent} for NiO; \SI{3.1}{\percent} at $|\vect{S}| = \frac52$, \SI{16}{\percent} at $|\vect{S}| = \frac12$}. Sum-rule defect $\Delta S/|\vect{S}|$ of the one-magnon weight: the full square roots of \cref{eq:7.1-HP} give $(|\vect{S}| - \Delta S)(1 + 2\Delta S)$ (\cref{eq:D.38-sumrule}) with $\langle S^z\rangle$ and $W_\parallel$ held at their linear spin-wave values, an estimate of the leading correction (the three-magnon part lowers the one-magnon weight further; the ground-state change at the same order is not included); the weight moves into the two-magnon continuum, $W_\parallel/W_\perp = 0.073$ for NiO (\cref{sec:14.2}). Also: magnetic form factor ($\times0.89$ at \SI{1}{MeV} in NiO), anisotropy gaps below \SI{50}{keV} ($\times29$ at \SI{10}{keV}, \SI{5}{meV} threshold, one-constant model, an upper value since only one branch of NiO reaches \SI{4.54}{meV}).
& \cref{sec:14.1} \\

Longitudinal and transverse spin weights (no box), \cref{eq:14.1-ratio}
& ---
& Published form: none for the ratio; zero-point reduction of the simple-cubic antiferromagnet~\cite{anderson1952approximate}. Ground state of the quadratic spin-wave Hamiltonian (vacuum of \cref{eq:D.19-bogo}): $\langle d^\dagger_{\mathrm{A}i}d_{\mathrm{A}i}\rangle = \Delta S$ (\cref{eq:D.38-Wperp}); Wick's theorem with vanishing anomalous average on one site $\to$ $W_\parallel = \Delta S(1 + \Delta S)$ (\cref{eq:D.38-Wpar}); $W_\perp = |\vect{S}|(1 + 2\Delta S)$. $\mathfrak{T}$: linear spin-wave theory, relative order $|\vect{S}|^0$; $T = 0$.
& Collinear two-sublattice antiferromagnet, $T \ll T_{\mathrm{N}}$ and $T \ll \omega^{\mathrm{M}}$; $\Delta S$ converged to $5\times10^{-5}$ on a $100^3$ grid (NiO). No Limit Check; \cref{we:14-nio} evaluates NiO ($W_\parallel/W_\perp = 0.0731$).
& \Fsize{unsized}. Magnon interactions of the next order in $1/|\vect{S}|$ and the change of the ground state at that order; the sum rule bounds neither weight separately (\cref{eq:D.38-sumrule}).
& \cref{sec:14.1} \\

Absorption into the uniform mode: the Larmor line (\cref{lim:14.3a}, panel a), \cref{eq:14.3-larmor,eq:14.3-rate}; $\lambda_S = g_J - 1$, \cref{eq:14.3-gJ}
& \cites[Eqs.~(2.4), (2.5)]{oshikawa2002electron}[Eqs.~(27), (30), (34), (35)]{mitridate2020detectability}[Eqs.~(25)--(27)]{chigusa2020detecting}
& Spin row of \cref{eq:8.1-alp} in \cref{eq:8.2-abs} $\to$ \cref{eq:14.3-abs}; $|\vect{p}_\phi|a \to 0$ and $F^{\mathrm{mag}}(0) = 1$, so $\vect{s}_e(0) = \sum_j\lambda_{S,j}\vect{S}_j$ (\cref{eq:14.3-s0}); exchange invariant under spin rotations, uniform $\lambda_S$ and $g_{\mathrm{ion}}$: $[H, S^-_{\mathrm{tot}}] = \omega_{\mathrm{Lar}}S^-_{\mathrm{tot}}$, weight $\langle S^+_{\mathrm{tot}}S^-_{\mathrm{tot}}\rangle = 2\langle S^z_{\mathrm{tot}}\rangle/(1 - \ee^{-\beta\omega_{\mathrm{Lar}}})$ $\to$ \cref{eq:14.3-larmor}. One sublattice at $T = 0$ gives the single-branch rate of Mitridate et al.; $g_{aee}/m_e = 1/f$, $\lambda_S = 1$ gives the golden-rule $(\pi/2)|V|^2\delta$ of Chigusa et al. Projection theorem within a $J$ multiplet $\to$ \cref{eq:14.3-gJ}. $\mathfrak{T}$: clamped nuclei, no electron--phonon vertex; exact in the magnetic many-body state, at every order in $1/|\vect{S}|$ and at every temperature. Leading correction on a qualifier, the target model (\cref{sec:9.4-place}): the shape of the sample, applied over demagnetizing field.
& Exact for any spin and temperature, ordered or paramagnetic, for a saturated sphere or an unbounded medium. Isotropic exchange, no anisotropy, uniform $\lambda_S$ and $g_{\mathrm{ion}}$. $\lambda_S = g_J - 1$ only within one $J$ multiplet; quenched $3d$ ions have $\lambda_S = 1 - O(\xi_{\mathrm{ion}}^2/\Delta_{\mathrm{CF}}^2)$ while $g_{\mathrm{ion}} - 2 = O(\xi_{\mathrm{ion}}/\Delta_{\mathrm{CF}})$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{10}{\percent} in line position and weight for $B_0 \ge 4.8\,\mu_0M_{\mathrm{s}}$ (thin film, field in plane); none for a saturated sphere}. Target model, the shape of the sample: demagnetization, \cref{eq:14.3-kittel,eq:14.3-ellipse}: the line moves to $\omega_{\mathrm{Kit}}$ and the transverse weights become elliptical, averaged factor $(\omega_{\mathrm{Kit},x} + \omega_{\mathrm{Kit},y})/2\omega_{\mathrm{Kit}}$ (1.74 for a YIG film at \SI{10}{\micro eV}, \cref{we:14-yig}); anisotropy fields move the line; nonuniform $\lambda_{S,j}$ gives gapped branches weight $\propto(\lambda_{S,j} - \lambda_{S,j'})^2$; non-ellipsoidal samples spread the weight over magnetostatic modes.
& \cref{sec:14.3} \\

Antiferromagnetic-resonance weight (\cref{lim:14.3a}, panel b), \cref{eq:14.3-afmr,eq:14.3-afmrweight}
& \cite[Eqs.~(2.4), (2.5)]{oshikawa2002electron} (Larmor statement, which this weight replaces)
& Published form: none for the weight; general multi-sublattice rate~\cite[Eq.~(27)]{mitridate2020detectability}. Two sublattices, easy axis, $\vect{k} = 0$ in \cref{eq:7.1-Hk} with $|\vect{S}|A_0 = \omega_E + \omega_A$, $|\vect{S}|B_0 = \omega_E$; \cref{eq:7.1-afm} $\to$ \cref{eq:14.3-afmr}; $S^x_{\mathrm{tot}}$ through the inverse of \cref{eq:7.1-bogo}, $(u^{\mathrm{M}}_0 - v^{\mathrm{M}}_0)^2 = \omega_A/\omega_{\mathrm{AFMR}}$ $\to$ \cref{eq:14.3-afmrweight}; axial field splits the line into $\omega_{\mathrm{AFMR}} \pm g\mu_B B_0$ with unchanged weights below the spin flop. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathfrak{T}_0$ with the magnetic reference state of linear spin-wave theory (relative order $|\vect{S}|^0$ in $1/|\vect{S}|$), clamped nuclei, $T = 0$. Leading correction on a qualifier, the target model (\cref{sec:9.4-place}): the spin-wave order and anisotropy over exchange.
& Linear spin-wave theory, $T = 0$, collinear two-sublattice order, uniaxial anisotropy along the field; $B_0$ below the spin flop $[B_A(2B_E - B_A)]^{1/2}$. Square-root form for $\omega_A \le 0.42\,\omega_E$.
& \Fsize{zero-point correction 5--\SI{23}{\percent} for $|\vect{S}| = \frac32$ at $\omega_A/\omega_E = 2$ to 0.3 (eight-site cluster); shape $\sim\omega_M/\omega_E = 1.6\times10^{-2}$ (MnF$_2$)}. Target model, the spin-wave order: zero-point fluctuations beyond linear spin-wave order (exact diagonalization of the eight-site easy-axis cluster in \cref{fig:14-limit-absorption}b); demagnetizing field of the small precessing net moment; for $|\vect{S}| = \frac52$ at small $\omega_A/\omega_E$ the zero-point correction is of order $1/2|\vect{S}| = 0.2$ and not sized in the book.
& \cref{sec:14.3} \\

& \cite[Eqs.~(8), (11), (26); dictionary Eq.~(B4)]{berlin2025determining}
& Magnetic neutron cross-section \cref{eq:C.19-cross} per formula unit $\to$ \cref{eq:14.4-PT}, with $\chi''_{ab} = 2(1 - \ee^{-\beta\omega})S_{ab}$ (a factor 4 from $\vect{\sigma} = 2\vect{s}$ and $\frac12$ from their $\pi/\Vol$ normalization) and $e^4/[8m_e^2(2\pi)^3] = r_{\mathrm{cl}}^2/4\pi$; quenched ion, $\vect{L} = (g_{\mathrm{ion}} - 2)\vect{S}$ at first order in spin--orbit coupling, $\lambda_S = 1$ to second order (\cref{sec:14.3}); dipole approximation (\cref{eq:C.19-dipole}), moment form factor $\langle j_0\rangle + (1 - 2/g_{\mathrm{ion}})\langle j_2\rangle$ $\to$ $C_{\mathrm{sn}}(q)$ of \cref{eq:14.4-data}; $C_{\mathrm{sn}} = 1$ $\to$ the published form. Thermal population removed by $1 - \ee^{-\omega/T_{\mathrm{d}}}$ (\cref{eq:4.2-fdt}, free magnons). $\mathfrak{T}$: none added; the response is measured, with its truncation that of the neutron coupling (Born approximation, spin--spin term).
& Transverse couplings only at one $\vect{q}$ without a model ($\mathfrak{D} \propto P_T$: $\mathcal{O}_9$, magnetic-dipole and anapole spin--spin terms); there $\mathcal{O}_4$ is bounded from below and the $\hat q_a\hat q_b$ couplings are not covered; within the localized-moment model, transverse data at several $\vect{q} + \vect{G}$ fix the full tensor (\cref{eq:14.4-Glat}, \cref{sec:14.4}). Spin-only reading within \SI{10}{\percent} for $g_{\mathrm{ion}} - 2 \le 0.094$ at $q \le \SI{5.3}{keV}$. Below $\mchi = \SI{134}{keV}$ every momentum transfer lies inside the first zone of YIG (inscribed radius \SI{708}{eV}), where the data are extrapolated (Berlin et al. to $q_{1\mathrm{BZ}} = 2\pi/a = \SI{987}{eV}$ for their $a$); phonon admixture grows as $(q/\SI{30}{keV})^2$. & \Fsize{$-$\SI{17}{\percent} for Ni$^{2+}$ at $g_{\mathrm{ion}} = 2.2$ ($C_{\mathrm{sn}}(0) = 0.83$); $-$\SI{27}{\percent} at $g_{\mathrm{ion}} = 2.34$}. Orbital moment of the ion: $C_{\mathrm{sn}}(0) = [\lambda_S/(g_{\mathrm{ion}}/2)]^2$; for $J$ multiplets $[(g_J - 1)/(g_J/2)]^2$, from $\frac19$ (Er$^{3+}$) to 25 (Sm$^{3+}$). The $\langle j_2\rangle$ term adds $-$\SI{2.7}{\percent} at \SI{5.3}{keV} and $-$\SI{9.6}{\percent} at \SI{10}{keV} (Ni$^{2+}$, $g_{\mathrm{ion}} = 2.2$).

& Published form: detection probability $\langle|\vect{L}^{\mathrm{ph}}|\rangle$ over the lowest band times the one-phonon rate. Moment \cref{eq:14.5-muph}: one-phonon expectation value of the two-phonon operator $\sum(eZ^*/2M)\vect{u}\times\vect{P}$. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{K}$ = one-phonon, $n_A = 0$, $T = 0$.
& GaAs (PBE force constants of \cref{sec:5.5}): rate-weighted lowest-band average within \SI{10}{\percent} of the band average for $\mchi \ge \SI{2.2}{MeV}$; inversion or time reversal broken. Decay does not conserve $\vect{L}^{\mathrm{ph}}$: $[H, L_z] = \frac{\ii}{2}\vect{u}\cdot[J_z, D]\vect{u}$. Plot (\cref{fig:14-limit-chiral}a): $x = \mchi$ from 0.1 to \SI{5}{MeV}; formula: band average 0.124; fuller: one-phonon-rate-weighted average for created lowest-band phonons and for all created phonons (heavy mediator, nucleon number, orientation-averaged kernel); shaded: lowest band within \SI{10}{\percent}.
& \Fsize{$-$\SI{10}{\percent} for $\mchi \ge \SI{2.2}{MeV}$ (GaAs, lowest band); below \SI{1}{\percent} of the rate in that band for $\mchi < \SI{0.8}{MeV}$; force constants 0.124 against 0.07, grid, masses and weighting $\le$\,\SI{14}{\percent}, long-range dipole part $-$\SI{14}{\percent} to $+$\SI{40}{\percent}}. Phonons created in the other branches (\SI{70}{\percent} of the one-phonon rate at large mass) and their three-phonon decay into the band, which changes $\vect{L}^{\mathrm{ph}}$ (\cref{app:E.19}, \cref{eq:E.19-branch}); the force constants.

& \cites[Eqs.~(1), (S.13)]{hochberg2021determining}[Eq.~(3.4)]{hochberg2016detecting}[Eqs.~(8)--(10)]{gan2026mean}[Eq.~(2) and text]{hochberg2023directional}
& Density row of \cref{eq:8.2-spectrum}, $S = (2q^2/e^2)L$ at $T = 0$ $\to$ \cref{eq:10.2-rate} with $\bar L \to L^{\mathrm{cons}}$; proper response = time-dependent mean field in $(\boldsymbol{\eta}, \mathsf{s}_1, \mathsf{s}_2)$, \cref{eq:7.2-Rc}, Coulomb as RPA in $\boldsymbol{\eta}$ $\to$ \cref{eq:15.1-Lcons}; constant $\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{F}}$, linear band, $\mathsf{s}_1$ decoupled by particle--hole symmetry $\to$ bubbles from the pair function $F_\mu$, \cref{eq:15.1-F,eq:15.1-bubbles} (\cref{app:D.39}), gap equation $\to$ \cref{eq:15.1-Rcons}; drop the ladder $\to$ bare bubble, $\Imag\varepsilon^{\mathrm{bare}} = \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}\Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}}$ exactly for $v_{\mathrm{F}}q \ge (\omega^2 - 4\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}^2)^{1/2}$, \cref{eq:15.1-gI}; normal real part $\to$ $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$; $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}} \to 0$ $\to$ $L^{\mathrm{nor}}$, whose unscreened form below $qv_{\mathrm{F}}$ is $m_e^2\omega/\pi q$. $\mathfrak{T}$: BCS reference, $\mathcal{X}$ = conserving generalized RPA (pairing ladder + Coulomb RPA), clean, quasiclassical, $T = 0$, nuclei clamped.
& $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}, k_{\mathrm{TF}}$; $\omega \gg c_{\mathrm{LA}}q$ (ionic response $(c_{\mathrm{LA}}/v_{\mathrm{max}})^2 = \num{6.6e-5}$ at the edge); electron mean free path $\gg \xi_{\mathrm{coh}}$ (1.6--\SI{2.4}{\micro m}) for the structure within $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ of the edge. ``Within \SI{10}{\percent}'' = smaller over larger $\ge 0.9$: Lindhard for $y \ge 7.9$ at $q = \omega/v_{\mathrm{max}}$ ($y \ge 6.3$ at four times that $q$); $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$ for $y \ge 2.25$; bare bubble for $y \ge 9.1$. Plot (\cref{fig:15-limit-chain}): $x = y$ at $q = \omega/v_{\mathrm{max}}$ from 2 to 50 (top axis $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/qv_{\mathrm{F}} = 0.392/y$); $L/L^{\mathrm{cons}}$ for bare bubble, its first-order form, $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$ and $L^{\mathrm{nor}}$, solid at the edge and dashed at four times the edge momentum; shaded $y \ge 7.9$.
& \Fsize{$\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}(y) - 1$: 0.15 at $y = 5$, 0.05 at $y = 10$; ladder: $L^{\mathrm{cons}}/(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}) \in [0.980, 1.085]$ for $y \ge 3$ (clean)}. Lindhard $\to$ superconductor: the case-I factor, $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}} - 1 \simeq (2\ln 2y - 1)/y^2$. Bare $\to$ conserving: the pair-field ladder, vertex fraction $+0.44$, $+0.21$, $+0.052$, $+0.004$ at the edge of $y = 2.2$, 3, 5, 10, with a shift of $\Real\varepsilon$ opposite to \cref{eq:15.1-sBCS}. Beyond the clean limit: impurity scattering (\cref{sec:15.3-sc}).
& \cref{sec:15.1} \\

First BCS correction to the screening (no box), \cref{eq:15.1-sBCS}
& ---
& Published form: none. $\delta\Imag\varepsilon = \Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}}[\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}\Theta(y - 2) - 1]$ from \cref{eq:15.1-gI}; $\Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}} = (\pi/2)(k_{\mathrm{TF}}^2/q^2)(\omega/qv_{\mathrm{F}})$ continued to $\omega \to \infty$; half-range Kramers--Kronig \cref{eq:4.3-KKhalf} $\to$ $\delta\Real\varepsilon = (k_{\mathrm{TF}}^2/q^2)(\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/qv_{\mathrm{F}})\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{BCS}}(y)$; $\Real\varepsilon \gg \Imag\varepsilon$ $\to$ $L^{\mathrm{bare}} \simeq \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}[1 - 2(\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/qv_{\mathrm{F}})\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{BCS}}]$. $\mathfrak{T}$: bare quasiparticle bubble with Coulomb RPA, clean, quasiclassical, $T = 0$; first order in $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/qv_{\mathrm{F}}$.
& $2(\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/qv_{\mathrm{F}})\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{BCS}} \ll 1$ and $\omega \ll qv_{\mathrm{F}}$. In halo kinematics the term is at most $2(v_{\mathrm{max}}/v_{\mathrm{F}})\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{BCS}}(y)/y$: \SI{3.9}{\percent} at $y = 10$, \SI{17}{\percent} at $y = 5$, \SI{53}{\percent} at $y = 3$, $>1$ at $y = 2.2$ (aluminum, $v_{\mathrm{max}}/v_{\mathrm{F}} = 0.392$). No Limit Check; it is the dotted curve of \cref{fig:15-limit-chain}.
& \Fsize{bare bubble at the edge 0.935 vs 0.961 ($y = 10$), 0.796 vs 0.835 ($y = 5$)}. Resummation of the screening near the edge (\cref{sec:15.2}); the pair-field ladder, which at the edge shifts $\Real\varepsilon$ by a comparable amount of the opposite sign and recovers the first-order coefficient only for $v_{\mathrm{F}}q \gtrsim 10^3\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ (0.954, 1.048, 1.053 against $\mathfrak{s}_{\mathrm{BCS}}(5) = 1.053$ at $v_{\mathrm{F}}q/\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}} = 10^2$, $10^3$, $10^4$).

Resummed loss function on the kinematic edge (\cref{lim:15.2a}), \cref{eq:15.2-LBCS,eq:15.2-cons}
& \cite[Eqs.~(8), (9)]{gan2026mean}
& Published form: $L = \Imag\varepsilon^{\mathrm{bare}}/|\varepsilon^{\mathrm{bare}}|^2$ of the bare BCS dielectric function, evaluated numerically. Absorptive part $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}\Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{Lin}}$, exact for $\omega < qv_{\mathrm{F}}$ (\cref{eq:15.1-gI}); real part shifted by the Kramers--Kronig term of \cref{eq:15.1-sBCS}; Lindhard function at $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}$, \cref{eq:15.2-Lin}, with $\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{L}}(\mathfrak{w})$ and $\mathfrak{r}_\varepsilon = \pi\mathfrak{w}/2\Real\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{L}}$ kept; $L = \Imag\varepsilon/|\varepsilon|^2$ without expansion $\to$ \cref{eq:D.39-resum} with $\mathfrak{g} = \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}$, $\delta = \delta_{\mathfrak{s}}$ $\to$ \cref{eq:15.2-LBCS}; first order in $\delta_{\mathfrak{s}}$ and $\mathfrak{r}_\varepsilon^2$ at $\omega \ll qv_{\mathrm{F}}$ $\to$ \cref{eq:15.1-sBCS}; conserving response \cref{eq:15.1-Rcons} $\to$ $\mathfrak{g} = \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}(1 + \mathfrak{v})$ and its own shift, \cref{eq:15.2-cons}. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ = bare quasiparticle bubble with Coulomb RPA (the box formula) and RPA with the pair-field ladder (the comparison); BCS mean-field reference, clean, quasiclassical, $T = 0$.
& $\omega < qv_{\mathrm{F}}$, $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}$, constant density of states; clean, $\mathfrak{l}_e \ge \SI{10}{\micro m}$ for the clean values to hold to \SI{4}{\percent} at $y \ge 2.2$. The bare form is at least 0.9 of the conserving one for $y \ge 9.1$ on the kinematic edge, and the Kramers--Kronig integral to infinity reproduces the directly evaluated bare bubble to \SI{1.5}{\percent}. Plot (\cref{fig:15-limit-edge}): $x = y$ from 2 to 30 on the edge $q = \omega/v_{\mathrm{max}}$ ($v_{\mathrm{max}}/v_{\mathrm{F}} = 0.392$); $y = L/L^{\mathrm{nor}}$; conserving (clean), bare (direct), resummed bare (Kramers--Kronig to infinity and cut at $qv_{\mathrm{F}}$), first order; conserving with Born impurities at $\mathfrak{l}_e = \SI{1}{\micro m}$ and \SI{30}{nm}; shaded $y \ge 9.1$.
& \Fsize{bare over conserving 0.38, 0.53, 0.75, 0.92 at $y = 2.2$, 3, 5, 10 on the edge}. The pair-field ladder, vertex fraction 0.44, 0.21, 0.052, 0.004 at the same $y$, whose shift of $\Real\varepsilon$ has the opposite sign; the spread of the Kramers--Kronig prescriptions, 0.33 to 0.40 of $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$ at $y = 2.2$. Edge remainder about $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$ two-sided, $-\SI{2}{\percent}$ to $+\SI{9}{\percent}$ for $y \ge 3$; energies $y < 3$ excluded (conserving value varies by more than \SI{10}{\percent} with $\mathfrak{l}_e$ from \SI{30}{nm} to \SI{100}{\micro m}). Beyond the clean limit: impurities, $L^{\mathrm{cons}}/\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}L^{\mathrm{nor}}$ at $y = 2.2$ from 0.87 (clean) to 0.19 (\SI{30}{nm}), within 1.008--1.091 at $y \ge 3$ (\cref{sec:15.3-sc}); local dirty limit \cref{eq:15.3-MB}.
& \cref{sec:15.2} \\

Drude and Mermin loss functions of a metal with collisions (no box), \cref{eq:15.3-limits,eq:15.3-mermin}
& \cite[Eq.~(19)]{knapen2021dark}; \cite[Eqs.~(168), (172)]{kahn2022searches}; \cite[Eqs.~(S.17), (S.18)]{hochberg2021determining}; \cite{mermin1970lindhard}
& Mermin function \cref{eq:4.5-mermin}; $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}$ in \cref{eq:4.5-lindhard} $\to$ $R^0 = -2\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{F}}\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{L}}(\mathfrak{w})$ (\cref{eq:15.2-Lin,eq:15.3-fL}) $\to$ \cref{eq:15.3-mermin}. Diffusive, $q\mathfrak{l}_e \ll 1$ (then $\omega \le qv_{\mathrm{max}} < qv_{\mathrm{F}} \ll \gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}$): $\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{L}} \simeq -1/3\mathfrak{w}_\gamma^2$ $\to$ diffusion pole \cref{eq:15.3-diff} $\to$ $L = \omega\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}/\omega_p^2$ for any $\omega/D_{\mathrm{diff}}q^2$, the Drude tail (the Fr\"ohlich form with core term 1 and no gap at $\omega \ll \omega_p$). Ballistic, $q\mathfrak{l}_e \gg 1$: $\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{L}} \simeq 1 + \ii\pi\mathfrak{w}_\gamma/2$ $\to$ the Lindhard corner $\pi\omega q/(2k_{\mathrm{TF}}^2v_{\mathrm{F}})$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}$-independent at first order. $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ = RPA for a homogeneous medium with number-conserving relaxation (not a many-body approximation), $n_B = 0$.
& $q \ll k_{\mathrm{F}}$, $q \ll k_{\mathrm{TF}}$; weak disorder, $k_{\mathrm{F}}\mathfrak{l}_e \gg 1$; $q d_{\mathrm{film}} \gg 1$ for bulk screening. Diffusive form within \SI{10}{\percent} for $q\mathfrak{l}_e \le 0.64$, ballistic form within \SI{10}{\percent} for $q\mathfrak{l}_e \ge 9.8$ ($\omega/\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}} = 0.034$). No Limit Check; \cref{fig:15-film}b shows the crossover.
& \Fsize{1.43 against the ballistic 0.79 at $q\mathfrak{l}_e = 1.5$; \SI{0.2}{\percent} for the Drude form at $q\mathfrak{l}_e = 0.086$}. The full crossover \cref{eq:15.3-mermin} between the limits; corrections of order $q/k_{\mathrm{F}}$; the Lindhard function at $\omega + \ii\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}$ gives twice the Drude tail at every $q\mathfrak{l}_e \ll 1$. Outside: strong disorder, $k_{\mathrm{F}}\mathfrak{l}_e \approx 1$ (amorphous WSi, \cref{sec:15.3-wsi}); sheet screening for $qd_{\mathrm{film}} \ll 1$, \cref{eq:15.3-sheet}; superconducting dirty film, \cref{eq:15.3-MB}.
& \cref{sec:15.3} \\

Phonon-assisted (Holstein) absorption of a superconductor (\cref{lim:15.4a}), \cref{eq:15.4-T,eq:15.4-holstein}
& \cite[Eqs.~(13), (16), (17)]{hochberg2016absorption}; Allen~\cite{allen1971electron}, as quoted there
& Current row at $\vect{p}_\phi \to 0$, through $\sigma_1$ in \cref{eq:10.7-dp}; one band, $\hat{\vect{e}}\cdot\op{\vect{V}} = \sum(\hat{\vect{e}}\cdot\vect{v}_{\vect{k}})\Psi^\dagger_{\vect{k}}\Psi_{\vect{k}}$ (\cref{eq:15.4-Vnambu}) commutes with $H_{\mathrm{MF}}$ $\to$ the second-order amplitude collapses exactly to \cref{eq:15.4-T} (\cref{eq:D.40-T}), case-I factor from the phonon vertex, $g_\nu(-\vect{k}-\vect{Q},\vect{Q}) = g_\nu(\vect{k},\vect{Q})$; $\sigma_1 = (\pi e^2/\omega\Vol)\sum_f|T_{fi}|^2\delta$; Fermi-surface approximation, independent $\xi$, $\xi'$ $\to$ $2x\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}(x/\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}})$; $\alpha^2F_{\mathrm{tr}}$ of \cref{eq:15.4-a2Ftr} $\to$ \cref{eq:15.4-holstein}; $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}} \to 0$ $\to$ Allen's normal-metal form; Debye $\alpha^2F_{\mathrm{tr}} = 2\lambda_{\mathrm{tr}}\omega'^4/\omega_{\mathrm{D}}^4$ $\to$ the published Eqs.~(16), (17). $\mathfrak{T}$: $n_g = 2$; BCS mean-field reference, $T = 0$; final pair without the ladder of \cref{eq:7.2-Rc}.
& $T = 0$; $\omega$ below the interband threshold (about \SI{0.5}{eV} in aluminum); pure crystal; weak coupling; ladder $(\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/v_{\mathrm{F}}Q)^2$, averaged over the phonon-assisted integrand, $4\times10^{-4}$ at $\omega = 2.2\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$. Normal-metal formula within \SI{10}{\percent} for $\omega \ge 11.4\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ ($\omega'^4$ law), $7.4\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ ($\omega'^2$ law). Plot (\cref{fig:15-limit-holstein}): $x = \omega/\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ from 2 to 400; $y = \sigma_1^{\mathrm{S}}/\sigma_1^{\mathrm{N}}$; case I with the $\omega'^4$ and $\omega'^2$ laws; the case-II factor and $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{II}}$ for comparison; formula 1; shaded $\omega \ge 11.4\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$.
& \Fsize{$\sigma_1^{\mathrm{S}}/\sigma_1^{\mathrm{N}} = 0.42$ ($\omega'^4$) or 0.73 ($\omega'^2$) at \SI{1}{meV}; $\times0.52$ from $1/(1 + \lambda_{\mathrm{tr}})^2$, known for the normal metal at $\omega \ll \omega_{\mathrm{D}}$~\cite[Eq.~(3)]{allen2015electron}, open for the superconducting form}. Shape of $\alpha^2F_{\mathrm{tr}}$ below a few meV (the plane-wave vertex of \cref{fig:6-a2F} fails the acoustic sum rule and puts $1.25\times10^4$ times the $\omega'^4$ weight below \SI{1}{meV}); strong coupling, pair weight $N^{\mathrm{El}}N^{\mathrm{El}\prime} + P^{\mathrm{El}}P^{\mathrm{El}\prime}$ (\cref{app:E.20}); electron--electron absorption, unsized; impurity-assisted absorption in films ($\times1.4\times10^6$ at \SI{1}{meV} for the \SI{50}{nm} film of \cref{we:15-film}).

Impurity-assisted (Mattis--Bardeen) absorption (no box), \cref{eq:15.4-MB,eq:D.40-g}
& \cite{mattis1958theory}
& Exact impurity eigenstates $\alpha$ with time-reversed partners $\bar\alpha$, same gap (\cref{sec:15.3}); current odd under time reversal $\to$ unit Nambu matrix $\to$ pair amplitude $u^{\mathrm{B}}_\alpha v^{\mathrm{B}}_\beta - v^{\mathrm{B}}_\alpha u^{\mathrm{B}}_\beta$ (case II); dirty limit $\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}} \gg \Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$: $|V^e_{\alpha\beta}|^2$ constant over a few $\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$, $\xi_\alpha$, $\xi_\beta$ independent $\to$ case-II integral of \cref{eq:7.2-pairspec} $= \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{II}}$, closed form \cref{eq:D.40-g}; no pair-field ladder (odd velocity against even pair vertices). $\mathfrak{T}$: first order in the current with exact impurity states; BCS mean field; $T = 0$.
& $\mathfrak{l}_e \ll \xi_{\mathrm{coh}}$; $T = 0$; below the interband threshold. $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{II}}$ within \SI{10}{\percent} of 1 for $\omega \ge 8.1\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$. No Limit Check; dotted curve of \cref{fig:15-limit-holstein}, \cref{fig:15-absorption}a.
& \Fsize{unsized}. Correlation of the momenta of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ when $\mathfrak{l}_e$ approaches $\xi_{\mathrm{coh}}$, where the clean limit (no absorption at $\vect{q} \to 0$) and the dirty limit meet; strong coupling as in the row above.
& \cref{sec:15.4} \\

Spin-current absorption in a clean superconductor (no box), \cref{eq:15.4-sJdef,eq:15.4-sJ}
& ---
& Published form: none; projections use \cref{eq:10.7-alp} with the phonon-assisted $\sigma_1$~\cite[Eq.~(22)]{hochberg2016absorption}. $\mathcal{J}^{zz}_e = \frac12\sum v^z_{\vect{k}}\Psi^\dagger_{\vect{k}}\boldsymbol{\eta}\Psi_{\vect{k}}$ (\cref{eq:D.40-O}); its part $(\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/E_{\vect{k}})\gamma^\dagger_{\vect{k}1}\gamma^\dagger_{\vect{k}2}$ creates pairs at $\vect{q} = 0$ (\cref{eq:D.40-bogo}); spin-rotation invariance, $S_{\mathcal{J}\mathcal{J}} = 3S_{\mathcal{J}^{zz}\mathcal{J}^{zz}}$; golden rule $\to$ \cref{eq:15.4-sJ}; integral $(\pi/2)(\omega_p^{\mathrm{intra}})^2$. Dirty limit: $\sigma_n\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}$. $\mathfrak{T}$: first order in the spin current; BCS mean field without spin--orbit coupling; $T = 0$.
& $\mathfrak{l}_e \gg \xi_{\mathrm{coh}}$ (1.6--\SI{2.4}{\micro m}: bulk high-purity crystals); no spin--orbit coupling; $T = 0$. Above the phonon-assisted $\sigma_1^{\mathrm{N}}$ up to \SI{10.9}{meV} in aluminum; $g_{aee} = 6.1\times10^{-13}$ with the factor $1/(1 + \lambda_{\mathrm{ep}})$ ($5.1\times10^{-13}$ bare) against $5.6\times10^{-9}$ (our recomputation with the phonon-assisted $\sigma_1^{\mathrm{S}}$) at \SI{0.72}{meV} for three events per kg-yr. Total weight exact (spin f-sum rule, zero spin stiffness of the singlet); coherent weight near the edge times $(1 + F_1^a/3)/(1 + \lambda_{\mathrm{ep}})$. Normal metal: spin current relaxed by electron--electron collisions, $\sigma_1^{\mathcal{J}} \approx 5$--\SI{45}{eV} at \SI{0.72}{meV} (estimate). No Limit Check; \cref{fig:15-absorption}b.
& \Fsize{$g_{aee} = 5.4$--$7.1\times10^{-13}$ for $|F_1^a/3| \le 0.1$ (Leggett form $(1 + F)(1 - f)/(1 + Ff)$)}. For $F_1^a < 0$ a bound spin-one $\ell = 1$ mode below $2\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$; triplet $p$-wave vertex; strong coupling beyond the residue; spin--orbit coupling, which mixes current and spin current; the phonon-assisted spin-current part near the edge. For $\mathfrak{l}_e \lesssim \xi_{\mathrm{coh}}$ the dirty form $\sigma_n\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}$ applies: for the \SI{50}{nm} film $g_{aee} = 1.5\times10^{-12}$ against $2.0\times10^{-12}$ from its Mattis--Bardeen $\sigma_1$.

& \cite[Eqs.~(2.6), (3.7), (3.11), (3.13), (3.14), (4.4), (4.5)]{hochberg2018detection}; \cite[Eqs.~(2.5)--(2.7), (2.25)--(2.27), (3.3), (3.13), (3.14)]{geilhufe2020dirac}
& Published form: vacuum $\sigmae$, pair transitions with a unit-charge vertex ($\Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}$), in-medium factor $1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{pub}}$ with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{pub}} = 1 + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}$; absorption with $\Pi = \omega^2(1 - \varepsilon_{\mathrm{pub}})$. Book: total-charge coupling, \cref{eq:8.4-exact} with the total $\varepsilon$; proper response split into Dirac bands and background, $1 - v_{\mathrm{C}}\tilde R_{\mathrm{bg}} \to \varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}$ (local, static) $\to$ \cref{eq:D.41-eps}; first-order $\vect{k}\cdot\vect{p}$ cone \cref{eq:7.3-H}, bubble \cref{eq:D.22-R} with a hard cutoff $\to$ \cref{eq:7.3-eps}; $L = \Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}/|\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}|^2$ $\to$ $L_{\mathrm{pub}} = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}^2L$ (\cref{eq:D.41-Lpub}), $\varGamma_{\mathrm{pub}} = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}\varGamma$ (\cref{eq:D.41-abspub}); anisotropic: $\hat{\vect{q}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}\cdot\hat{\vect{q}}$ against the per-component factor (\cref{eq:D.41-ratio}), $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg},ii}^2$ on the axes. The source's Eq.~(3.14), and the $00$ entry of its Eq.~(3.13), print $q_x^2v_z^2/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg},xx}$ for $q_x^2v_x^2/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg},xx}$; its table column of gaps holds the half-gap $\Delta_{\mathrm{D}}$ (text below its Table~1). $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ = RPA for the Dirac electrons; static local background; $T = 0$, chemical potential in the gap.
& Published form within \SI{10}{\percent} only for $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}} \le 1.049$ (scattering), $1.10$ (absorption). Plot (\cref{fig:16-limit-dirac}): $x = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}$ from 1 to 1000 (log); $y$ = rates over their value at $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}} = 1$; scattering at $m_\chi = \SI{100}{keV}$ (light mediator) and absorption at $m_\phi = \SI{50}{meV}$, gapped benchmark $v_{\mathrm{D}} = 4\times10^{-4}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{D}} = \SI{2.5}{meV}$, $g_{\mathrm{deg}} = 2$, $\Lambda_{\mathrm{D}} = \SI{0.2}{keV}$; shaded $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}} \le 1.049$; hatched $\tilde\alpha < 1$.
& \Fsize{published form $\times\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}^2$ (scattering), $\times\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}$ (absorption): 1600 and 40 at $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}} = 40$; 5200--6400 for ZrTe$_5$ scattering (measured-structure row, $\Delta_{\mathrm{D}} = \SI{11.8}{meV}$, book halo); 4800--6600 over the rows at 11.8 and \SI{15.6}{meV} and the halos (238, 250.5, 544) and (220, 232, 600)~km\,s$^{-1}$; 4300 with $v_0 = 220$, $v_{\mathrm{esc}} = \SI{544}{km.s^{-1}}$}. Of the total-$\varepsilon$ form: the electron--hole ladder, Sommerfeld factor $x/(1 - \ee^{-x})$, $x = \pi\tilde\alpha/b$, above 1.5 for pair energies below 1.37 times the edge in ZrTe$_5$ (\SI{60}{\percent}, \SI{12}{\percent}, \SI{3}{\percent} of the rate at 20, 100, \SI{1000}{keV}, measured-structure row); self-energy and vertex of relative order $\tilde\alpha$ with an unfixed coefficient; leading weak-screening form $\Imag\varepsilon_{\mathrm{D}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{bg}}^2$ overstates the ZrTe$_5$ rate by 6--\SI{16}{\percent}; $\tilde\alpha \gtrsim 1$ open.

One-dimensional plasmon of linear channels (\cref{lim:16.2a}), \cref{eq:16.2-wire,eq:16.2-kernel1D}
& \cite[Eqs.~(3)--(6)]{zhang2026luttinger}
& Published form: $S_{\mathrm{1D}} = g|q|\delta(\omega - v|q|)$ with $g = [(2\pi v_{\mathrm{F}} + g_4 - g_2)/(2\pi v_{\mathrm{F}} + g_4 + g_2)]^{1/2}$, one right- and one left-moving mode. Density row; embedding \cref{eq:16.2-embed} with $F_\perp = J_0(q_\perp r_{\mathrm{t}})$; $N_{\mathrm{ch}}$ linear channels, forward density--density coupling $g_2 = g_4 = v_{\mathrm{C}}^{\mathrm{1D}}$ (\cref{eq:16.2-kernel1D}); random-phase response, exact for this model $\to$ \cref{eq:16.2-wire}. The charge mode carries all channels; the $N_{\mathrm{ch}} - 1$ neutral modes do not couple. The source has $N_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ with the measured four-channel $g_c$: its $S_{\mathrm{1D}}$ and rate (Eq.~(6)) are low by 4 at fixed $g_c$ (by 2.1 at a fixed kernel, $q_\parallel = \SI{10}{eV}$). $\mathfrak{T}$: $\mathcal{X}$ = RPA, $d = 1$, $n_B = 0$.
& Linear bands: $q_\parallel$ small against the scale $k_{\mathrm{F}}$ of band curvature; $\omega < 2v_{\mathrm{D}}/r_{\mathrm{t}} = \SI{1.7}{eV}$ (next subbands) and $q_\parallel \ll 1/r_{\mathrm{t}}$ for a nanotube; independent tubes ($q_\perp$ above the inverse spacing); $T = 0$, no backscattering. Plot (\cref{fig:16-wire-limit}): $x = q_\parallel/k_{\mathrm{F}}$ (0.01--1.9, log); $y$ = plasmon frequency and pole share of the $f$-sum for $N_{\mathrm{ch}} = 4$ parabolic channels over the linear-band values; kernel of a tube with $r_{\mathrm{t}} = \SI{0.7}{nm}$, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{env}} = 1$; $v = v_{\mathrm{D}} = 3.02\times10^{-3}$; $k_{\mathrm{F}} = 1/r_{\mathrm{t}} = \SI{282}{eV}$ (model); shaded $q_\parallel < 1.36\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{10}{\percent} for $q_\parallel < 1.36\,k_{\mathrm{F}}$; $+$\SI{4.0}{\percent} in $\omega_{\mathrm{pl}}$ at $q_\parallel = k_{\mathrm{F}}$}. Band curvature, $\omega_{\mathrm{pl}}^{\mathrm{par}}/\omega_{\mathrm{pl}} = 1 + (q_\parallel^2/2k_{\mathrm{F}}^2)g_c^2[\frac14 + g_c^2/3(1 - g_c^2)]$, with weight lost to the pair band (pole share 0.97 at $k_{\mathrm{F}}$); then the subbands at $2v_{\mathrm{D}}/r_{\mathrm{t}}$, the Coulomb interaction between tubes of a forest, backscattering, and damping of the plasmon by contacts and disorder.
& \cref{sec:16.2} \\

The first source's text writes $\delta\varepsilon = \gamma u_{\mathrm{d}}$, while its Eq.~(7) holds for $\delta\varepsilon = 2\gamma u_{\mathrm{d}}$; with $\delta\varepsilon = \gamma u_{\mathrm{d}}$ the rate would be four times smaller (\cref{app:D.42}). Plot (\cref{fig:16-limit-heat}): $x = t/\tau_{\min}$ from $10^{-2}$ to $10^{18}$ (log); $y = t\dot Q_{\mathrm{TLS}}$ over the prefactor, model with $\lambda_{\mathrm{tun}}$ cut over a range 14, $\Delta_0 \le 0.1\,T_{\mathrm{lo}}$, exact $\Delta_{\mathrm{tl}}$, $T_{\mathrm{lo}}/T_{\mathrm{hi}} = 1/480$; shaded where within \SI{10}{\percent}.
& \Fsize{$\le$\,\SI{10}{\percent} for $1.8\,\tau_{\min} \le t \le 7.1\times10^{10}\,\tau_{\min}$ (10.6 of 12.2 decades); within $2\times10^{-4}$ from $10^2$ to $10^8\,\tau_{\min}$}. The ends of the distribution in $\lambda_{\mathrm{tun}}$; finite cooling rate (freezing, \cref{eq:16.8-Tfrz}); measured $\bar P$ from heat release four times below the specific-heat value in vitreous silica (stress-lowered barriers); interactions and avalanches (\cref{sec:16.8-open}), unsized.
& \cref{sec:16.8} \\
\midrule
Partial (valence) f-sum of the loss function (\cref{lim:17.5a}), \cref{eq:17.5-partial}
& \cite[Table~I]{ramanathan2020ionization} (measured $\omega_p^{\mathrm{val}}$)
& Published form: $\int_{\mathrm{val}}\omega L\,\dd\omega = (\pi/2)(\omega_p^{\mathrm{val}})^2$. Chain: f-sum of the loss function \cref{eq:4.4-fsumL}; $\varepsilon = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{val}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}} - 1$ with additive groups (no Pauli transfer between shells), valence absorption below the first core edge, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(\omega) \to \varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0)$ (Kramers--Kronig, \cref{eq:C.5-KKeps}) over the valence spectrum; the large-frequency tail of $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{m}}^{-1} - 1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0)$, with the model function $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{m}} = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0) + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{val}} - 1$ (equal to $\varepsilon$ below the core edge to $O(\omega^2/\omega_{\mathrm{core}}^2)$; for the true $\varepsilon$ an intermediate asymptote), gives $(\pi/2)(\omega_p^{\mathrm{val}})^2/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0)^2$. $\mathfrak{T}$: clamped nuclei, $q \to 0$, exact response.
& Upper limit between the valence spectrum and the first core edge. Silicon: within \SI{10}{\percent} for $\omega_c$ from \SI{32.7}{eV} ($2\omega_p^{\mathrm{val}}$) to the L edge at \SI{99.8}{eV}; 1.000 just below the edge. Plot (\cref{fig:17-limit-a}): $x = \omega_c$, 3~eV--30~keV (log); $y$ = running $\int_0^{\omega_c}\omega L$ and $\int_0^{\omega_c}\omega\varepsilon_2$ over $(\pi/2)(\omega_p^{\mathrm{val}})^2$ from measured optical constants (Franta et al., below \SI{35}{eV}) and the Henke tables above; dashed the formula, dotted with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0) = 1.0154$; shaded the valid range.
& \Fsize{$-$\SI{3}{\percent} core screening, $+$\SI{3}{\percent} shell transfer (Si)}. Core screening $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{core}}(0)^{-2} = 0.970$; oscillator strength moved from blocked core transitions into the valence region ($\varepsilon_2$-sum 1.032 below the edge); both known to the \SI{1.4}{\percent} normalization of the data; at finite $q$ valid only while $qv_{\mathrm{max}} < \omega_{\mathrm{core}}$.
& \cref{sec:17.5} \\

Pair energy and Fano factor as cascade moments (\cref{lim:18.2a}), \cref{eq:18.2-moments,eq:18.2-decomp}
& \cite[Eqs.~(2)--(3)]{ramanathan2020ionization}
& Published form: $\varepsilon(\omega) = \omega/\bar n(\omega)$ and $F(\omega) = \operatorname{Var}N_{\mathrm{eh}}/\bar n$ at each deposit, constant at high energy. Chain: energy identity \cref{eq:18.1-energy} (no capture, excitons or escaping photons before counting) $\to$ mean and variance at fixed $\omega$ \cref{eq:18.2-moments} (\cref{eq:D.43-moments}); heat split $E_{\mathrm{heat}} = E_< + E_>$ $\to$ \cref{eq:18.2-decomp} (\cref{eq:D.43-decomp}); additive deposits solved by \cref{eq:D.43-m1,eq:D.43-m2} under the energy-only random-$k$ reduction (\cref{sec:18.1-reductions}); large-$\omega$ slopes, existence of the leading term \cref{eq:18.2-linear} (\cref{app:D.44}), constant terms $b_N$, $c_N$ fitted. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector.
& Silicon model ($\Eg = \SI{1.160}{eV}$, $\omega_{\mathrm{AB}} = \SI{63}{meV}$, $A_{\mathrm{AB}} = \SI{5.703}{eV^3}$, $\theta_{\mathrm{ii}} = 1$, $B_{\mathrm{h}} = \SI{12}{eV}$, one-sided seed): both ratios within \SI{10}{\percent} of the limits for $\omega \ge 14.9\,\Eg = \SI{17.3}{eV}$. Plot (\cref{fig:18-limit-moments}): $x = \omega/\Eg$ from 1 to 60 (log); $y = \varepsilon(\omega)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$ and $F(\omega)/F_{\mathrm{eh}}$; dashed with the constant terms $b_N = 0.35$, $c_N = -0.02$; shaded $\omega \ge 14.9\,\Eg$.
& \Fsize{$b_N\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}/\omega$: \SI{10}{\percent} at $\omega = 10\Eg$; residual after it $\le$\,\SI{0.9}{\percent} there}. Constant terms of the mean and variance (not derived; \cref{sec:18.2-open}); periodic remainder from the lattice of losses in a one-phonon model; step structure below $15\Eg$.
& \cref{sec:18.2} \\

Linear mean yield (no box), \cref{eq:18.2-linear,eq:18.2-tagged}
& \cite[Chap.~XI]{feller1971introduction} (key renewal theorem)
& Tagged-unit identity \cref{eq:D.44-tagged} (energy conservation and Markov property only) $\to$ walk of $\ln x$ along the line of one unit; scale-free share law approached as $(\Eg/x)^\varkappa$ (Dirichlet law without a hole cutoff; Beta law of the two electrons with the cutoff, where the phonon ratio falls as $x^{-3/2}$), non-lattice, finite mean, regular near threshold $\to$ coupling to the scale-free walk and key renewal theorem for its undershoot (\cref{app:D.44}). $\mathfrak{T}$: detector.
& Any energy-conserving Markov cascade satisfying the four conditions of \cref{app:D.44}; random-$k$ cascade with $\varkappa = 1$. Fails for fixed-proportion splits (log-lattice).
& \Fsize{unsized}. Constant term $b_N$ (0.35 in silicon, one-sided seed; \SI{13}{\percent} of $\bar n$ at \SI{10}{eV}) and the variance law need second-order renewal estimates (\cref{sec:18.2-open}).
& \cref{sec:18.2} \\

Random-parking (R\'enyi) limit (\cref{lim:18.2b}), $\rho_\infty$ and $F_{\mathrm{park}}$
& \cite[Eqs.~(2), (19), (23), (44)]{subashiev2010correlation}
& Published form: $\rho_\infty = \int_0^\infty\ee^{-2\,\mathrm{Ein}(t)}\dd t$, $F = 0.0510387$, $F_{\mathrm{unc}} = \operatorname{Var}g/(1 + \langle g\rangle)^2$. The source prints $\rho_\infty = 0.7475987$ (the integral, at 30 digits: 0.747597920) and $F_{\mathrm{unc}} = 0.0439766$, a dropped digit of 0.04439766, as its own $F_{\mathrm{nnp}} = F_{\mathrm{unc}} + 0.007685 = 0.05208$ requires (the printed value would give 0.05166). Chain: random-$k$ cascade (\cref{eq:18.1-randomk}) with constant electron density of states, delta-function hole density of states, $\theta_{\mathrm{ii}} = 1$, $\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}} \to 0$ $\to$ Rényi parking of unit cars; mean recursion by Laplace transform (\cref{we:18-parking}); second moment by \cref{eq:C.11-second}. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector.
& Within \SI{10}{\percent} (both $\Eg/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$ and $F_{\mathrm{eh}}$) for $\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}} < 0.40$ at $w_{\mathrm{AB}} = 0.0543$. Plot (\cref{fig:18-limit-park}): $x = \lambda_{\mathrm{AB}}$ from $10^{-3}$ to 10 (log); $y = F_{\mathrm{eh}}/F_{\mathrm{park}}$, $(\Eg/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}})/\rho_\infty$, $F_{\mathrm{ren}}/F_{\mathrm{park}}$; grid step $\Eg/74$; shaded $\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}} < 0.40$.
& \Fsize{$\pm0.8\,\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}}$ for $\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}} \le 0.003$; $-$\SI{3.2}{\percent} ($\rho$), $+$\SI{3.7}{\percent} ($F$) at 0.1}. Phonon emission above threshold; the parabolic density of states moves the $\lambda_{\mathrm{AB}} \to 0$ values to $1.585\,\Eg$ and 0.081.
& \cref{sec:18.2} \\

Renewal (uncorrelated final-state) Fano estimate (\cref{lim:18.2c}), \cref{eq:18.2-ren,eq:18.2-rencasc}
& \cite[Eq.~(7)]{subashiev2010correlation}
& Published form: $F_{\mathrm{unc}} = \langle\delta\epsilon^2\rangle/\epsilon^2$. Chain: independent, identically distributed costs per pair, single renewal chain \cref{eq:C.11-fano} with the energy constraint ($\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$ in the denominator) $\to$ \cref{eq:18.2-ren}; costs built from one terminal electron and one terminal hole with the computed residual variances and Poisson phonon heat $\to$ \cref{eq:18.2-rencasc}. Compared with the full \cref{eq:18.2-decomp}. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector.
& Lower value of $F_{\mathrm{eh}}$ in every computed case (ratio 0.870 parking; 0.696, 0.837 parabolic without phonons at $\theta_{\mathrm{ii}} = 1$, $3/2$; 0.193 Si; 0.418 Si at $\theta_{\mathrm{ii}} = 3/2$; 0.126 Ge, hole band cut at \SI{12.6}{eV}); nowhere within \SI{10}{\percent}; the general bound is open (\cref{sec:18.2-open}). Plot (\cref{fig:18-limit-renewal}): $x = \lambda_{\mathrm{AB}}$ from $10^{-3}$ to 30 (log); $y = F_{\mathrm{ren}}/F_{\mathrm{eh}}$; band 0.9--1 empty.
& \Fsize{$F_{\mathrm{eh}}/F_{\mathrm{ren}} = 1.15$ (parking) to 5.2 (Si), 7.9 (Ge)}. All correlations between carriers (siblings, electron and hole of a pair, distant carriers), non-identical cost laws, and the non-Poisson phonon heat with its covariance $2\mathcal{V}_{<>}$.
& \cref{sec:18.2} \\

Step model and Ramanathan--Kurinsky model as limits of the seeded cascade distribution (\cref{lim:18.3a}), \cref{eq:18.3-step,eq:18.3-beta,eq:18.3-pq}
& \cites[Eqs.~(5)--(10), (14)]{ramanathan2020ionization}[Eq.~(5.1)]{essig2016direct}
& Published form: $n(\omega) = 1 + \int_0^{\omega - \Eg}\dd E\,P(E,\omega)\langle N(E)\rangle$ with $P(E,\omega)$ the Beta split of the electron share, and $N_{\mathrm{eh}} = 1 + \lfloor(\omega - \Eg)/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}\rfloor$. Chain: hand-off \cref{eq:8.7-cond} $\to$ seed distribution \cref{eq:18.3-seed} with the energy-only reduction applied after the first collisions (first-generation momenta dropped) $\to$ \cref{eq:18.3-pcasc,eq:18.3-pq} from the product form \cref{eq:18.1-seed}; seed replaced by a process-independent, electron--hole symmetric split \cref{eq:18.3-beta} fitted to photon yields; one yield function for electrons and holes; random-$k$ kernels with free-particle densities of states and one phonon (\cref{eq:18.1-randomk}), $\theta_{\mathrm{ii}} = 1$, hole energies below the valence bandwidth $\to$ the source's Eqs.~(5), (8), (10); large-$\omega$ renewal limit with the constant term of the mean set to zero $\to$ its Eq.~(14). Step model: every pair after the first costs exactly $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$, deterministic renewal chain (\cref{app:C.11}) $\to$ \cref{eq:18.3-step}, Fano factor zero. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector.
& Prompt pair number independent of the seed. Silicon (cascade with $A_{\mathrm{AB}} = \SI{5.703}{eV^3}$, $\Eg = \SI{1.160}{eV}$): step model within 0.1 in $d_{\mathrm{TV}}$ of the dark-matter seed for $\omega < \SI{4.37}{eV}$; splits with $\alpha_{\mathrm{RK}} = 1$ and $\to\infty$ also for $\omega > \SI{12.95}{eV}$. Plot (\cref{fig:18-limit-seed}): $x = \omega$ from 1.4 to \SI{21.2}{eV}; $y = d_{\mathrm{TV}}$ to the cascade seeded by the dark-matter kick at $q_0$ (empirical-pseudopotential pair weights), for the step model, the splits $\alpha_{\mathrm{RK}} \to \infty$, 1, $\to 0$ and the photon seed; shaded the two regions.
& \Fsize{$d_{\mathrm{TV}} \le 0.062$ (energy-only model, one $\vect{k}$ grid; unresolved in the full band, where the photon seed moves 0.11--0.14 between grids) for a photon seed in place of the dark-matter seed (largest at \SI{5.67}{eV}; mean \SI{4.6}{\percent}); splits with $\alpha_{\mathrm{RK}} = 1$, $\to\infty$ up to 0.21, 0.39, one-sided split 0.67; step model up to 0.86}. Leading correction: the momentum-transfer dependence of the seed (photon against dark-matter shares); then the constant term of the mean, 0.008 (uniform split) to 0.35 (one-sided seed), which decays in $d_{\mathrm{TV}}$ only as $\omega^{-1/2}$; first-generation valley and momentum memory, dropped by the energy-only reduction (negligible within the deformation-potential model of a full-band cascade). Against that full-band cascade at matched pair energy: energy-only cascade 0.12--0.20 at 6--\SI{8}{eV}; splits $\alpha_{\mathrm{RK}} = 1$, $\to\infty$ 0.11--0.12, 0.45--0.50; step model 0.79--0.83.
& \cref{sec:18.3} \\

& ---

Valence density $32/a^3$ (four electrons per atom). $\mathfrak{T}$: a support statement, independent of the order in the ion's charge (Born parameter $Z_\kappa\alpha/v_{\mathrm{F}} = 15$ in silicon).
& Valence electrons no faster than $v_{\mathrm{F}}$; binary collision with one electron; $v_N \ll v_{\mathrm{F}}$ (silicon: $v_N = v_{\mathrm{F}}/43$ at threshold). No Limit Check; \cref{fig:19-yield} marks the threshold against the silicon data.
& \Fsize{$-$\SI{4.4}{\percent} (Si), $-$\SI{3.1}{\percent} (Ge) from the $v_N^2$ term; momentum tail unsized}. Keeping $v_N^2$ gives \SI{336}{eV} and \SI{387}{eV}. The valence momentum tail beyond $k_{\mathrm{F}}$ makes the threshold soft: in the pseudopotential model of silicon $5.6\times10^{-3}$ of the valence electrons can still be promoted at \SI{100}{eV} (\cref{eq:19.2-pmin}), an understatement of the true tail. & \cite[Eqs.~(3)--(7)]{sarkis2020study}; \cite[Eqs.~(5)--(7)]{sorensen2015atomic}; \cite[Eq.~(3)]{agnese2018nuclear}; \cite{lindhard1963integral,lewin1996review}
& Published form: $y_L = k_Lg_L/(1 + k_Lg_L)$, $g_L = 3\epsilon_L^{0.15} + 0.7\epsilon_L^{0.6} + \epsilon_L$, $k_L = 0.133Z^{2/3}A^{-1/2}$, $\epsilon_L = 11.5Z^{-7/3}E_R/\si{keV}$. Conversion $Y_q = y_L$ assumes $N_{\mathrm{eh}} = y_LE_R/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$, full collection, no trapping. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector (classical binary transport; electronic loss first order in the electronic transfer, coefficient fitted).
& $\epsilon_L \gtrsim 0.1$ ($E_R \gtrsim \SI{4}{keV}$ in silicon), where the formula describes the silicon data. Fit against our solution of \cref{eq:19.3-simple} (Thomas--Fermi impulse cross-section, $x_L^{1/3}$ below $x_L = 2\times10^{-3}$): within \SI{3}{\percent} below \SI{100}{eV}, \SIrange{4}{5}{\percent} low from 1 to \SI{20}{keV}, within \SI{5}{\percent} for $\epsilon_L \ge 0.1$. Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-lindhard}): $x = E_R$, 0.01--\SI{100}{keV} (log; top axis $\epsilon_L$); $y$ = formula over solution, band for the two continuations of the cross-section; data over formula; constant-binding model over formula; shaded $\epsilon_L \ge 0.1$; line at $E_{R,\mathrm{th}}$.
& \Fsize{$-$8 to $-$\SI{38}{\percent} (data over formula, 0.1--\SI{3.9}{keV}); fit within 3--\SI{5}{\percent} of its own equation}. Below a few keV: stored energy (\cref{sec:19.4}), the binary threshold and near-gap conversion (\cref{sec:19.2}), trapping (\cref{sec:19.5}); the solution is sensitive to the continuation of the cross-section below $x_L = 2\times10^{-3}$: $+$\SI{13}{\percent} at \SI{1}{keV} for the local slope 0.363, $-$\SI{11}{\percent} and $+$\SI{40}{\percent} for exponents 0.30 and 0.40; the $\epsilon_L^{1/6}$ law follows from the $x_L^{1/3}$ continuation (the Thomas--Fermi tail $x_L^{1/2}$ keeps $y_L$ finite). Constant binding $U = \SI{0.15}{keV}$ cuts the yield off at \SI{298}{eV}, against data at \SI{100}{eV}. Modified models book the binding of secondaries as ionization, and their $\frac12k_L\epsilon_L^{3/2}\epsilon_{\mathrm{d}}''$ term contradicts (D).
& \cref{sec:19.3} \\

Bethe stopping from the loss function (\cref{lim:19.3b}), \cref{eq:19.3-bethe,eq:19.3-lnI}
& \cite[Eq.~(34.5), Sect.~34.2.1]{navas2024review}
& Published form: $-\dd E/\dd x = Kz^2(Z/A)\beta^{-2}[\frac12\ln(2m_e\beta^2\gamma^2W_{\mathrm{max}}/I^2) - \beta^2 - \delta/2]$. Chain: dielectric stopping \cref{eq:4.4-dEdx} (first order in $Z_1$, heavy projectile, $T = 0$) $\to$ split at $q_0$, with $q_0v$ above the loss spectrum and $q_0$ below the inverse atomic size $\to$ optical limit $L(0,\omega)$ below $q_0$, f-sum \cref{eq:4.4-fsumL} saturated from $q_0$ to the Bethe ridge $2m_ev$ above $\to$ $q_0$ cancels $\to$ \cref{eq:19.3-bethe} with $\ln\bar I$ \cref{eq:19.3-lnI}; the source's bracket reduces to $\ln(2m_ev^2/I) + O(v^4)$ for $W_{\mathrm{max}} = 2m_e\beta^2\gamma^2$, $\delta = 0$. $\mathfrak{T}$: first Born, $n = 1$ in $Z_1$.
& $2m_ev^2 \gg \bar I$ and $Z_1\alpha/v \ll 1$. Electron gas of the silicon valence density (Mermin, relaxation $0.1\omega_p$, $\bar I = \SI{17.9}{eV}$): within \SI{10}{\percent} for $v \ge 1.84v_{\mathrm{F}}$, \SI{5}{\percent} for $v \ge 2.27v_{\mathrm{F}}$, \SI{1}{\percent} above $4.3v_{\mathrm{F}}$. Silicon $\bar I$ from the measured loss function \SI{161}{eV}, adopted \SI{173.0}{eV}. Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-bethe}): $x = v/v_{\mathrm{F}}$, 0.02--30 (log; top axis $Z_1\alpha/v$ for $Z_1 = 14$); $y = -\dd E/\dd x$ for $Z_1 = 1$; dielectric, Bethe, friction; shaded the \SI{10}{\percent} region; band of silicon recoils.
& \Fsize{$\le$\SI{10}{\percent} for $v \ge 1.84v_{\mathrm{F}}$}. Shell corrections (the f-sum not exhausted at $q_0$), Barkas ($Z_1^3$) and Bloch terms, density effect (relativistic). Silicon recoils ($v = 0.013$--$0.13\,v_{\mathrm{F}}$, $Z_1\alpha/v = 117$--1170) lie in the friction regime, where $\bar I$ plays no role.
& \cref{sec:19.3} \\

Displacement count and defect energy loss (\cref{lim:19.4a}), \cref{eq:19.4-count,eq:19.4-KP,eq:19.4-loss}
& \cite[Eq.~(1)]{nordlund2018improving}
& Published form: $N_{\mathrm{FP}} = 0$ for $T_{\mathrm{d}} < E_{\mathrm{d}}$, 1 for $E_{\mathrm{d}} \le T_{\mathrm{d}} < 2E_{\mathrm{d}}/0.8$, $0.8\,T_{\mathrm{d}}/2E_{\mathrm{d}}$ above (Norgett--Robinson--Torrens, as quoted). Chain: partition \cref{eq:19.1-partition} $\to$ mean stored energy over end basins \cref{eq:19.4-mean} $\to$ binary collisions with uncorrelated targets at rest (\cref{sec:19.3}) and the Kinchin--Pease rules (displaced above $E_{\mathrm{d}}$; replacement when the projectile keeps less than $E_{\mathrm{d}}$), no binding energy, electronic losses removed so that the argument is $T_{\mathrm{d}}$ $\to$ first-collision balance \cref{eq:19.4-count} $\to$ hard spheres: \cref{eq:19.4-KP} exactly; Thomas--Fermi cross-section of \cref{eq:19.3-dsigma} ($x_L^{1/3}$ below $x_L = 2\times10^{-3}$, i.e.\ $\dd\sigma \propto T^{-4/3}\dd T$ near threshold): $\xi_{\mathrm{d}} = 0.70$ $\to$ times $p_{\mathrm{surv}}E_{\mathrm{FP}}$: \cref{eq:19.4-loss}. $\mathfrak{T}$: adiabatic ($n_B = 0$), classical binary transport.
& Linear range $T_{\mathrm{d}} \ge 2.5E_{\mathrm{d}}$; binary, uncorrelated collisions, random medium. Screened count: $2E_{\mathrm{d}}N_{\mathrm{FP}}/T_{\mathrm{d}} = 0.72$ at $10E_{\mathrm{d}}$, $0.70$ ($\pm3\times10^{-3}$ for $E_{\mathrm{d}} = 12.5$--\SI{36}{eV}) above $100E_{\mathrm{d}}$; a direct Monte Carlo of the same rules (power law) agrees within 1.6 standard errors. Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-nrt}): $x = T_{\mathrm{d}}/E_{\mathrm{d}}$, 1--1000 (log); $y = 2E_{\mathrm{d}}N_{\mathrm{FP}}/T_{\mathrm{d}}$; screened ($E_{\mathrm{d}} = 36$, \SI{12.5}{eV}), power law, hard spheres, formula; shaded $T_{\mathrm{d}} \ge 2.5E_{\mathrm{d}}$.
& \Fsize{$+$11 to $+$\SI{14}{\percent} (formula over screened count, $T_{\mathrm{d}} \ge 10E_{\mathrm{d}}$); $-$\SI{20}{\percent} against hard spheres}. The factor depends on cross-section and counting rule. A binding energy per displacement and recombination in dense cascades both lower the count (metals: a third to a quarter of the formula). Below $2.5E_{\mathrm{d}}$ the direction dependence of $E_{\mathrm{d}}$ and the zero-point width of the step (\SI{0.72}{eV} at \SI{12.5}{eV}, \cref{sec:19.1}) set $p_{\mathrm{FP}}$. Read through \cref{eq:19.4-loss}, the stored-energy bounds of \cref{lim:19.5b} give $p_{\mathrm{surv}}E_{\mathrm{FP}}/E_{\mathrm{d}} \le 0.19$ (Si, \SI{10}{keV}), 0.27 (Si, \SI{100}{keV}), $\ge 0.23$ (Ge, not conservative) with the factor 0.8.
& \cref{sec:19.4} \\

Unit heat quenching (\cref{lim:19.5a}), $Y_{\mathrm{ph}}(0) = 1$; \cref{eq:19.5-heatq,eq:19.5-release}
& \cite[p.~4]{alkhatib2021light}
& Published form: the heat quenching factor, the ratio of the heat signals of nuclear and electron recoils of equal energy, set to one. Chain: balance law \cref{eq:19.5-balance} (complete reservoirs, carrier energies from $\mu_F$, configurational $E_{\mathrm{st}}$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{zp}}$ in the phonons) $\to$ signal $=$ efficiency $\times$ (deposit $-$ missing energy at $t_{\mathrm{win}}$), absolute efficiency cancels $\to$ \cref{eq:19.5-heatq} $\to$ gap energy of the pairs written with the returned fraction $f_{\mathrm{ret}}(t_{\mathrm{win}})$, \cref{eq:19.5-release} $\to$ $\delta_{\mathrm{coll}} = 0$, $f_{\mathrm{ret}} = 1$ for both recoils, $E_{\mathrm{st}} = E_{\mathrm{esc}} = \Delta E_{\mathrm{zp}} = 0$. $\mathfrak{T}$: detector (energy bookkeeping, no expansion).
& All pairs recombined or collected with their gap energy returned as phonons within the window, no stored, trapped or escaped energy, equal collection. Silicon, \SI{100}{eV} ($Y_q = 0.102$): within \SI{1}{\percent} of one for a held fraction $1 - f_{\mathrm{ret}} \le 0.035$ (0.036 at \SI{0.89}{keV}, 0.041 at \SI{3.91}{keV}). Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-heat}): $x = f_{\mathrm{ret}}$ from 0 to 1; $y = Y_{\mathrm{ph}}(0)$ for the measured $Y_q$ at 0.10, 0.89, \SI{3.91}{keV}, and at \SI{0.10}{keV} with $E_{\mathrm{st}} = 0.05\,\omega$; shaded $f_{\mathrm{ret}} \ge 0.965$; marker at the asymptotic $f_{\mathrm{ret}} = 0.941$ of the illustrative kinetics of \cref{fig:18-end}.
& \Fsize{$+$\SI{41}{\percent} at $f_{\mathrm{ret}} = 0$ (\SI{100}{eV}); $+$\SI{1.7}{\percent} for \SI{5.9}{\percent} of the gap energy trapped or radiated}. Gap energy held at the end of the window, $(1 - Y_q)(\Eg/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}})(1 - f_{\mathrm{ret}})$ at first order, invalid beyond small $1 - f_{\mathrm{ret}}$ (first order 1.280 against exact 1.407 at $f_{\mathrm{ret}} = 0$); stored energy $-E_{\mathrm{st}}(t_{\mathrm{win}})/\omega$, of opposite sign; collection offset $-\delta_{\mathrm{coll}}$.
& \cref{sec:19.5} \\

Stored energy from the phonon-yield ratio (\cref{lim:19.5b}), \cref{eq:19.5-identity,eq:19.5-first,eq:19.5-bound,eq:19.5-widen}
& \cite[Eq.~(4), Table~II]{agnese2018energy}; \cite[Eqs.~(1), (4), Sects.~2, 3.2, 4]{agnese2018nuclear}
& Published form: $f_{\mathrm{DF}} = 1 - Y_{\mathrm{ph}}$ read as stored energy, with $E_R = E_\phi - E_QeV_{\mathrm{bias}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}}$ on a phonon scale from bulk electron recoils of unit yield, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}} = \SI{3.82}{eV}$; Si $Y_{\mathrm{ph}} = 0.952^{+0.009}_{-0.007}$ (\SI{95}{\percent} C.L.), Ge $f_{\mathrm{DF}} = (6.08 \pm 0.18)\,\%$. Chain: balance law \cref{eq:19.5-balance} with bias work \cref{eq:19.5-Wbias} $\to$ primary and Luke phonons collected with $\epsilon_{\mathrm{prim}}$, $\epsilon_{\mathrm{Luke}}$; Luke phonons follow the true $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$ ($g_{\mathrm{Luke}}$), scale and subtraction the adopted $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}}$ ($g_{\mathrm{cal}}$), signals \cref{eq:19.5-signal} $\to$ exact identity \cref{eq:19.5-identity}, calibration identity \cref{eq:19.5-calib} $\to$ first order \cref{eq:19.5-first} with $C_{\mathrm{cal}}$ $\to$ exact bound identity \cref{eq:19.5-bound} (\cref{app:D.45}) $\to$ (a) $\delta_{\mathrm{coll}} \ge 0$, (b) $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} \ge 1$, (c) other missing energy of the nuclear recoil at least $(1 + f_{\mathrm{ion}}g_{\mathrm{cal}})/(1 + g_{\mathrm{cal}})$ times that of the electron recoil, (d) $E_{\mathrm{st}}/\omega \le 1 - f_{\mathrm{ion}}$, (e) $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}} \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}}$ $\Rightarrow$ $E_{\mathrm{st}}/\omega \le f_{\mathrm{DF}}$ $\to$ \SI{95}{\percent} C.L.: lower edge of the Si interval; Ge $\hat f + 1.645\sigma$ (\cref{app:C.14}). $\mathfrak{T}$: detector (bookkeeping with multiplicative collection).
& Electron recoils fully collected; one efficiency per phonon population; calibration energies only (Si 10--\SI{100}{keV}, \SI{4}{V/cm}; Ge $^{206}$Pb at \SI{103}{keV}). Bounds 0.055 (Si; 0.049 under (a)--(d) with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}} = \SI{3.75}{eV}$, attained at $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} = 1$), 0.064 (Ge); the sign of $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} - 1$ is not fixed by the balance law; the mean-field transport estimate gives 0.81--1.02 with energy conserved (0.82--1.01 at the mean Luke energy of silicon electrons at \SI{1}{V/cm}), up to 1.5 only for a selective sink not identified in a detector, so collection alone (1.128) is not reached by it but not excluded until computed kernels test real detectors (\cref{sec:20.5-sign}, \cref{app:E.25}); the two-bias measurement decides. Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-yield}): $x = \mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}}$ from 0.8 to 1.3; (a) $Y_{\mathrm{ph}}$ at 4 and \SI{40}{V} for $E_{\mathrm{st}} = 0$ and $0.042\,\omega$ ($f_{\mathrm{ion}} = y_C(\SI{20}{keV}) = 0.324$, band 0.269--0.497), measured band, dot at $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} = 1.128$; (b) \SI{95}{\percent} bound on $E_{\mathrm{st}}/\omega$ from \cref{eq:19.5-widen}, the same with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}} = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$, Ge point; shaded $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} \ge 1$.
& \Fsize{$-0.0064$ in $Y_{\mathrm{ph}}$ from $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cal}} \ne \varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}}$ (Si, \SI{4}{V}); bound 0.081 at $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} = 0.9$; first order against exact $\le 3.0\times10^{-3}$ for deficits in $[0, 0.05]$}. Second-order terms $\delta_{\mathrm{coll}}d_{\mathrm{NR}}$ and those in $d_{\mathrm{ER}}$. On $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} < 1$ the inferred stored energy grows linearly, \cref{eq:19.5-widen}. The datum is degenerate: collection alone ($\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} = 1.128$), stored energy alone (0.042), or a gap offset with $\rho_g - 1 = 0.21$ each reproduce it. A negative zero-point change widens the bounds by about $10^{-3}$ (0.056, 0.065). Polarity-dependent trapping in Ge (detectors differ by 2.06 standard deviations).
& \cref{sec:19.4,sec:19.5} \\

Gap-energy release law (no box), \cref{eq:19.5-release,eq:19.5-bimol}
& ---
& Published form: none. Chain: \cref{eq:19.5-heatq} with the pair energy at $t_{\mathrm{win}}$ written through the returned fraction $f_{\mathrm{ret}}(t)$; linear pair kinetics of \cref{eq:18.5-rates} give $1 - f_{\mathrm{ret}}$ as a sum of exponentials, equal for nuclear and electron recoils; in dense tracks two-body loss, $\dd\nd/\dd t \propto -\nd^2$ (exciton annihilation) or $-\nd^3$ (Auger), gives \cref{eq:19.5-bimol}. $\mathfrak{T}$: rate equations, first order in each process.
& Linear law: dilute pairs, every transition first order in the pair's own state, zero field. Power laws: no diffusion during the window ($4D_Xt_{\mathrm{win}}$ small against the squared track radius), no linear decay competing.
& \Fsize{unsized}. Diffusion of the track (saturating two-body loss); electron--hole droplets; the rates at millikelvin, none measured (\cref{sec:18.5-open}); excitons hold $\Eg - E_x^b$, an offset of at most \SI{1.7}{\percent} of the held energy in silicon.
& \cref{sec:19.5} \\

Two-bias inversion (no box), \cref{eq:19.5-linear,eq:D.45-inversion}
& ---

Defecton diffusion in a phonon gas (\cref{lim:19.6a}), \cref{eq:19.6-q2,eq:19.6-D}
& \cite[Eqs.~(12), (49)--(61), (64)]{pushkarov2004quantum}
& Published form: $D_T = (a\bar c/3\alpha_{\mathrm{P}}\sigma_{\mathrm{P}})[\varepsilon(p)/\theta_p]^2(\theta_p/T)^9$, $\theta_p = \bar c/2a$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{P}} = 1.27$. Chain: narrow tight-binding band \cref{eq:19.6-band} ($A_{\mathrm{dfn}}a \ll \bar c$) $\to$ one-phonon absorption and emission kinematically forbidden $\to$ two-phonon (Rayleigh) scattering, amplitude $\propto\omega$ for both the quadratic coupling and the linear coupling at second order (the second difference of the band survives) $\to$ collision rate $\propto T^7$, momentum change per collision $\sim T/\bar c \ll 1/a$, $\langle q^2\rangle \propto T^9$ \cref{eq:19.6-q2} $\to$ crystal momentum diffusing at a constant rate $D_1 = \langle q^2\rangle/6$ with the detailed-balance drift, separable band, Green--Kubo integral solved in closed form $\to$ \cref{eq:19.6-D}, $D_9 = \Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}^2/12\langle q^2\rangle$; equals the published form for $\varepsilon(p) = \Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}/4$. $\mathfrak{T}$: defect on the Born--Oppenheimer surface, phonons at second order in the coupling ($n_B = 2$ in the rate), dilute defectons.
& $\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}} \ll T \ll \theta_p$ as published; in the band model within \SI{10}{\percent} for $T \ge 0.28\,\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$, \SI{1}{\percent} for $T \ge 0.93\,\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$; no strain gradient with $|\nabla\phi|a > \Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$ (localization radius $R_0 = a(V_0/\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}})^{1/3}$); defecton--defecton scattering negligible. Plot (\cref{fig:19-limit-defecton}): $x = T/\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$, $10^{-2}$--$10^{1.5}$ (log); $y = D/D_9$; band model, effective-mass law, leading correction; shaded $T \ge 0.28\,\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\SI{10}{\percent} for $T \ge 0.28\,\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$}. The bracket of \cref{eq:19.6-D} expands as $1 - \frac{5}{576}(\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}/T)^2$; at $T \ll \Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}/6$ the law becomes $6T^2/\langle q^2\rangle \propto T^{-7}$ (twice the coefficient of the published estimate, Eq.~(64)); local exponent $-7.9$ at $T = 0.1\,\Delta_{\mathrm{dfn}}$. Above $\theta_p$ the Rayleigh law fails. The band model assumes a $\vect{k}$-independent momentum diffusion.
& \cref{sec:19.6} \\

Crowdion profile and kink mass (\cref{lim:19.6b}), \cref{eq:19.6-Mcr}; \cref{eq:C.20-kink,eq:C.20-Ekink,eq:C.20-EPN}
& \cite[Eqs.~(2), (3)]{fitzgerald2018structure}; \cite{braun2004frenkel}
& Published form: $u(z) = (2a/\pi)\arctan\ee^{-\mu(z - z_0)}$, $E_0 = (2a/\pi)(2V_0\beta)^{1/2}$ with $\mu = 1/d_{\mathrm{kink}}$, $\beta = K_{\mathrm{ch}}$, $V_0 = V_{\mathrm{s}}$. Compared with the discrete chain relaxed numerically at the minimum and the saddle. $\mathfrak{T}$: classical static chain on a model substrate potential; Born--Oppenheimer.
& $d_{\mathrm{kink}} \gg a_{\mathrm{ch}}$; sinusoidal substrate potential. All three quantities within \SI{10}{\percent} of the discrete chain for $d_{\mathrm{kink}} \ge 1.74\,a_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (set by $E_{\mathrm{PN}}$); energy within \SI{10}{\percent} above $0.57\,a_{\mathrm{ch}}$, mass above $0.75\,a_{\mathrm{ch}}$. & \Fsize{$-$\SI{1.4}{\percent} in $E_{\mathrm{kink}}$ at $d_{\mathrm{kink}} = a_{\mathrm{ch}}$; $E_{\mathrm{PN}}$ $-$\SI{22}{\percent} there, $-$\SI{7.5}{\percent} at $2a_{\mathrm{ch}}$}. Energy: the $k^4$ term of the discrete spring energy, $-E_{\mathrm{kink}}a_{\mathrm{ch}}^2/72d_{\mathrm{kink}}^2$, followed within $2\times10^{-4}$ above $1.5\,a_{\mathrm{ch}}$; mass $+$\SI{3}{\percent} at one spacing. Hecht collection (\cref{lim:20.1a}), \cref{eq:20.1-hecht}; \cref{eq:20.1-hecht2,eq:20.1-qavg}
& \cite{hecht1932mechanismus}, \cite[Eqs.~(1)--(4)]{lachish2017electron}
& Published form: $Q/Q_0 = (\mu\tau V/d^2)(1 - \ee^{-d^2/\mu\tau V})$. Chain: Shockley--Ramo theorem \cref{eq:20.1-ramo} with the planar weighting potential $z/d_{\mathrm{det}}$ $\to$ induced charge proportional to the drifted path, \cref{eq:20.1-qpair} $\to$ independent capture events: survival $\ee^{-s/\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}}$ along the path $\to$ charge created at one face, one carrier type crosses $\to$ mean cut-off path, \cref{eq:20.1-hecht} $\to$ $\lambda_{\mathrm{d}} = v_{\mathrm{dr}}\tau_{\mathrm{tr}}$ with $v_{\mathrm{dr}} = \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E}$, $\mathcal{E} = V_{\mathrm{bias}}/d_{\mathrm{det}}$ $\to$ published form. Bulk events: both carriers, \cref{eq:20.1-hecht2}, depth average \cref{eq:20.1-qavg}. $\mathfrak{T}$: incoherent classical drift after the prompt cascade; independent capture events.
& Exponential survival (trap occupation fixed during the drift); uniform field; for the voltage form, $v_{\mathrm{dr}} \propto \mathcal{E}$ and a field-independent $\tau_{\mathrm{tr}}$. With the saturating interpolation $v_{\mathrm{dr}} = \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E}/(1 + \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E}/v_{\mathrm{sat}})$, $v_{\mathrm{sat}}\tau_{\mathrm{tr}} = 2d_{\mathrm{det}}$: within \SI{1}{\percent} for $\mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E} \le 0.0101\,v_{\mathrm{sat}}$, \SI{10}{\percent} for $\le 0.117\,v_{\mathrm{sat}}$. Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-hecht}): $x = \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\tau_{\mathrm{tr}}V_{\mathrm{bias}}/d_{\mathrm{det}}^2$, $10^{-2}$--$10^2$ (log); $y$ = collected fraction over the published form; saturating drift and leading correction; shaded $\mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E} \le 0.1\,v_{\mathrm{sat}}$.
& \Fsize{$-\mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E}/v_{\mathrm{sat}}$ (relative) at low bias; $-$\SI{8.9}{\percent} at $\mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E} = 0.1\,v_{\mathrm{sat}}$}. From $\lambda_{\mathrm{d}} = \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\tau_{\mathrm{tr}}\mathcal{E}(1 - \mu_{\mathrm{mob}}\mathcal{E}/v_{\mathrm{sat}})$; at high bias the collection saturates at $\kappa(1 - \ee^{-1/\kappa})$, $\kappa = v_{\mathrm{sat}}\tau_{\mathrm{tr}}/d_{\mathrm{det}}$ (0.787 at $\kappa = 2$). With a capture cross-section per unit path independent of speed, $\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}$ does not depend on the bias.
& \cref{sec:20.1} \\
