Bias work and the Luke phonon energy (\cref{lim:20.1b}), \cref{eq:20.1-work}; \cref{eq:19.5-Wbias}
& \cite[Eq.~(1)]{agnese2018nuclear}
& Published form: $E_\phi = E_R + n_QeV_b$, Luke part $n_QeV_b$. Chain: Green's reciprocity $\to$ Shockley--Ramo theorem \cref{eq:20.1-ramo} $\to$ work of the sources $\sum_kV_k\Delta Q_k$ $\to$ \cref{eq:20.1-work} with the vacuum potential $\varphi_{\mathrm{bias}}$ of the electrodes (exact with space charge) $\to$ two planar electrodes: $eV_{\mathrm{bias}}\mathfrak{q}$ per pair, \cref{eq:19.5-Wbias} $\to$ trapping, depth average \cref{eq:20.1-qavg} $\to$ full collection, $\mathfrak{q} = 1$ $\to$ published form. Luke heat $= W_{\mathrm{bias}} - \Delta E_{\mathrm{es}}$ (\cref{app:D.45}). $\mathfrak{T}$: classical electrostatics in a linear dielectric; quasistatic drift.
& Two planar electrodes (with more, the read charge follows $\varphi_s$ and the work $\varphi_{\mathrm{bias}}$); $\Delta E_{\mathrm{es}}$ negligible (\SI{1.3e-5}{eV} for a pair \SI{10}{\micro m} apart in silicon). Within \SI{1}{\percent} for $d_{\mathrm{det}} \le 0.030\,\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}$ (equal drift lengths), $d_{\mathrm{det}} \le 0.015\,\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^e$ ($\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^h = \lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^e/3$). Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-work}): $x = d_{\mathrm{det}}/\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^e$, $10^{-3}$--10 (log); $y = W_{\mathrm{bias}}/N_{\mathrm{eh}}eV_{\mathrm{bias}}$; two drift-length ratios with leading corrections; shaded $d_{\mathrm{det}} \le 0.030\,\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}$.
& \Fsize{$-d_{\mathrm{det}}/6\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^e - d_{\mathrm{det}}/6\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^h$; $-$\SI{3.3}{\percent} at $d_{\mathrm{det}} = 0.1\,\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}$ (equal drift lengths)}. Next order $+d_{\mathrm{det}}^2/24\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}^2$ per carrier, so the leading term is accurate to a fraction $d_{\mathrm{det}}/4\lambda_{\mathrm{d}}$ of itself. The planar reconstruction subtracts the Luke heat of partly collected pairs exactly through the read charge.
& \cref{sec:20.1} \\
\midrule
Fano factor of the measured charge (\cref{lim:20.1c}), \cref{eq:20.1-Fpos}; \cref{eq:C.11-thinfano}
& \cite[Eqs.~(A6)--(A7)]{ramanathan2020ionization}
& Published form: $F_{\mathrm{meas}} = F/\eta + (1 - \eta)$, from the variance $Fn + \eta(1 - \eta)n$ over the mean $\eta n$, with $\eta$ the collection efficiency ($= f_{\mathrm{det}}$). Chain: induced charge per pair $\mathfrak{q}_i \in [0, 1]$, independent and identically distributed given the event position and independent of $N_{\mathrm{eh}}$ $\to$ compound sum at fixed position $\to$ law of total variance over the position $\to$ \cref{eq:20.1-Fpos} $\to$ all-or-nothing, position-independent collection: binomial thinning $fF_{\mathrm{eh}} + 1 - f$, \cref{eq:C.11-thinfano} $\to$ published form, which leaves the pair variance unscaled by $\eta^2$. A Monte Carlo of $4\times10^5$ events (binomial pair number, mean 26.6, Fano factor 0.114) agrees with \cref{eq:20.1-Fpos} to 0.0004. $\mathfrak{T}$: carriers drift independently after the prompt cascade (no recombination or screening in the track).
& Published form within \SI{10}{\percent} of exact thinning for $f_{\mathrm{det}} \ge 0.923$; exact thinning needs all-or-nothing loss with one probability everywhere. At fixed position $\sigma_{\mathfrak{q}}^2 \le \bar{\mathfrak{q}}(1 - \bar{\mathfrak{q}})$, so partial charges give less than thinning. Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-fano}): $x = f_{\mathrm{det}}$, 0.5--1; $y = F_{\mathrm{meas}}$ at $F_{\mathrm{eh}} = 0.114$; published form, thinning, midplane partial charges, uniform depth at $\bar N_{\mathrm{eh}} = 26.7$; shaded $f_{\mathrm{det}} \ge 0.923$.
& \Fsize{$+F_{\mathrm{eh}}(1/f_{\mathrm{det}} - f_{\mathrm{det}})$ against thinning, 0.012 at $f_{\mathrm{det}} = 0.95$}. The source's quoted values, 0.130 at 98\,\% and 0.160 at 95\,\%, lie close to exact thinning (0.133 and 0.159 at $F = 0.115$; the printed form gives 0.137 and 0.171). Beyond thinning: partial charges lower $F_{\mathrm{meas}}$ at fixed position (0.125 against 0.158 at 95\,\%), and depth-dependent collection adds the position term, $1.22\times10^{-4}\bar N_{\mathrm{eh}}$ at 95\,\% for uniform depth (\cref{we:20-fano}).
& \cref{sec:20.1} \\
\midrule
Down-conversion and the scaling spectrum (\cref{lim:20.2a}), \cref{eq:20.2-anh,eq:20.2-Estar,eq:20.2-scaling}; \cref{eq:20.2-frag}
& \cite[Eqs.~(6), (8), Table~I]{leman2012invited}; \cite[Sect.~2.2.2]{kelsey2023g4cmp}; \cite[Tables~3, 4]{hernandez2025modeling}
& Published form: $\varGamma_{\mathrm{A}} = A\nu^5$ (longitudinal), $\varGamma_{\mathrm{I}} = B\nu^4$, with the statement that the details of a high-frequency birth distribution are rapidly lost. Chain: three-phonon linewidth \cref{eq:5.7-gamma} at $T = 0$ $\to$ acoustic sum rule removes the zeroth order of the vertex in each wavevector, \cref{eq:20.2-V3} $\to$ phase space $\propto\omega^2$ $\to$ \cref{eq:20.2-anh}; transverse decay closed in the isotropic continuum $\to$ isotope mixing faster than longitudinal decay ($B_{\mathrm{iso}}/A_{\mathrm{anh}} = \SI{33}{THz}$ in Si) $\to$ one mean rate $f_{\mathrm{LA}}\varGamma_{\mathrm{anh}}$ $\to$ energy-conserving fragmentation \cref{eq:20.2-frag} with a self-similar daughter distribution $\to$ one scale $E_*(t)$, \cref{eq:20.2-Estar} $\to$ scaling form \cref{eq:20.2-scaling}. Compared with the solution of \cref{eq:20.2-frag} for three hard births (line at \SI{62}{meV}, $E^3$ spectrum, optical-decay pairs), $P_{\mathrm{d}}(y) = 60y^3(1 - y)^2$, exact matrix exponential on a logarithmic grid of step 0.01. Mean energy $0.667\,E_*$; median $0.66\,E_*$, \SI{80}{\percent} of the energy within 0.36--$0.97\,E_*$. $\mathfrak{T}$: three-phonon decay at lowest order ($n_A = 2$ in the rate), isotope scattering at second order in the mass defects, incoherent transport, $n_T = 0$.
& $t \gg 1/(f_{\mathrm{LA}}A_{\mathrm{anh}}f_{\mathrm{top}}^5)$ (\SI{0.19}{ns} Si, \SI{1.3}{ns} Ge); isotope mixing faster than decay (fails for sapphire, $B_{\mathrm{iso}}/A_{\mathrm{anh}} = 0.02$--\SI{0.21}{THz}); no surfaces or absorption; continuum rates (the transverse branches of Si are linear only below \SI{1.4}{THz}, \cref{lim:20.2b}). Mean energy within \SI{10}{\percent} of the scaling law for $t \ge \SI{0.46}{ns}$ for all three births. Memory: births above $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$ agree to $10^{-3}$ once $E_* \le 0.51$--$0.53\,E_{\mathrm{mem}}$. Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-scaling}): $x = t$, $3\times10^{-3}$--$10^4$~ns (log); $y = \langle E_{\mathrm{ath}}\rangle/0.667E_*$; three births and the undecayed line; shaded $t \ge \SI{0.46}{ns}$.
& \Fsize{$\le$\SI{10}{\percent} for $t \ge \SI{0.46}{ns}$ (Si)}. Before the first decays the mean is the birth energy, $\langle E_{\mathrm{ath}}\rangle/0.667E_* = (f_{\mathrm{LA}}A_{\mathrm{anh}}f_0^5t)^{1/5}/0.667$ for a line at $f_0$. The numbers rest on $A_{\mathrm{anh}}$ (Si: 7.41--$\SI{11.5e-56}{s^4}$, $E_*$ changes by 0.92; Ge: $\times10$ spread, factor 0.63) and on $f_{\mathrm{LA}}$ (0.07--0.10, $E_*$ within \SI{6}{\percent}); the shape constants depend on $P_{\mathrm{d}}$, here a symmetric stand-in for the isotropic-continuum distribution of \cref{app:E.12}.
& \cref{sec:20.2} \\
\midrule
Rayleigh law of isotope scattering (\cref{lim:20.2b}), \cref{eq:20.2-rayleigh}; \cref{eq:20.2-isoborn,eq:E.12-iso}
& \cite[Eq.~(4), Table~4]{hernandez2025modeling}
& Published form: $\varGamma_{\mathrm{iso}} = B_{\mathrm{iso}}\nu^4$, $B_{\mathrm{iso}} = 4\pi^3g_{\mathrm{md}}\Omega_{\mathrm{at}}\langle c^{-3}\rangle$ (Tamura's result; written with $\Gamma_{\mathrm{md}}$ and $1/\langle c^3\rangle$ in the source). Chain: mass defects about the mean mass, mass variance $g_{\mathrm{md}}$ $\to$ self-energy of the mass disorder at lowest order (\cref{sec:11.6}); cubic site symmetry makes the width independent of the initial mode $\to$ \cref{eq:20.2-isoborn} $\to$ Debye density of states $3\Omega_{\mathrm{at}}\omega^2\langle c^{-3}\rangle/2\pi^2$ $\to$ published form. Compared with \cref{eq:20.2-isoborn} for the PBE density of states of \cref{sec:5.2}, integrated on rays from $\mathrm{\Gamma}$ (32 polar, 64 azimuthal points, 800 radial steps, \SI{0.1}{meV} bins), Debye reference with the PBE sound speeds. Si: $g_{\mathrm{md}} = 2.007\times10^{-4}$, $\langle c^{-3}\rangle = \SI{4.90e-12}{s^3.m^{-3}}$ (measured tensor), $B_{\mathrm{iso}} = \SI{2.44e-42}{s^3}$ (measured 2.42--2.56, polarization spread). $\mathfrak{T}$: second order in the mass defects, harmonic lattice with mass-independent force constants.
& Wavelengths long against the lattice spacing; independent isotopes; single scattering (pair terms of \cref{sec:11.6} below \SI{2.2}{\percent} to \SI{55}{meV} in natural Si). Within \SI{10}{\percent} for $E_{\mathrm{ath}} \le \SI{5.6}{meV}$ (\SI{1.36}{THz}, $0.10\,\omega_{\mathrm{D}}$). Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-iso}): $x = E_{\mathrm{ath}}$, 0--\SI{30}{meV}; $y = \varGamma_{\mathrm{iso}}$ over the Rayleigh law; leading correction; shaded $E_{\mathrm{ath}} \le \SI{5.6}{meV}$.
& \Fsize{$+9.6\,(E_{\mathrm{ath}}/\omega_{\mathrm{D}})^2$; $+$\SI{5}{\percent} at \SI{1}{THz}, $+$\SI{10}{\percent} at \SI{5.6}{meV}}. The transverse branches bend over: the density of states exceeds the Debye form, up to a factor 8.1 at the van Hove peak near \SI{17.6}{meV}. The PBE $\langle c^{-3}\rangle$ is \SI{22}{\percent} above the measured one (soft transverse branches); the absolute rate uses the measured tensor.
& \cref{sec:20.2} \\

Transfer-kernel bound on observable differences (no box), \cref{eq:20.2-bound}
& ---
& Published form: none; the statement that only the soft fraction of the birth spectra survives transport. Chain: linear transport, observable linear in the delivered energies with weights in $[0, 1]$ $\to$ response $R_O(E)$ to unit energy born at $E$ $\to$ no absorption before $t_{\mathrm{mem}}$: responses to births above $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$ differ by at most $\Delta_{\mathrm{h}}(t_{\mathrm{mem}})$ $\to$ $\int\Delta\hat\rho_0 = 0$, subtract the midpoint of the response above $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$ $\to$ \cref{eq:20.2-bound}; $= \Delta_{\mathrm{soft}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{h}}$ when $\Delta\hat\rho_0$ has one sign below $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$. Ratio observables linearized. $\mathfrak{T}$: as the scaling spectrum; transport linear in the deposit (occupations $\sim4\times10^{-9}$).
& Linear transport; no sensor absorption before $t_{\mathrm{mem}}$ (bulk events farther than $r_{\mathrm{rms}}(t_{\mathrm{mem}})$ from an instrumented face: \SI{68}{\micro m} at \SI{100}{ns}, \SI{0.54}{mm} at \SI{1}{\micro s} in Si); $\Delta_{\mathrm{h}}$ from the scaling solution, $E_{\mathrm{m}} \ge E_*(t_{\mathrm{mem}})/0.52$.
& \Fsize{bound 0.15 against the largest model difference 0.012 at \SI{1}{\micro s} (Si, the births of \cref{fig:20-spectrum}); it reduces to $f_{\mathrm{s}} = 0.017$ only if $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$ lies below every hard birth}. With $E_{\mathrm{m}} = \SI{21.6}{meV}$ the bound counts the part of the cubic spectrum below $E_{\mathrm{mem}}$ as well as the soft fraction; a ratio observable adds its linearization error, quadratic in the difference.
& \cref{sec:20.2} \\
\midrule
Impedance law as the averaged interface transmission (\cref{lim:20.3a}), \cref{eq:C.18-transmission}; \cref{eq:20.3-avg,eq:20.3-recip}
& \cite{swartz1989thermal}
& Published form: $t_{12}(0) = 4Z_{\mathrm{ac},1}Z_{\mathrm{ac},2}/(Z_{\mathrm{ac},1} + Z_{\mathrm{ac},2})^2$. Chain: welded plane interface between isotropic media (Voigt averages) $\to$ conserved $Q_\parallel$, \cref{eq:20.3-Qz} $\to$ SH in closed form \cref{eq:20.3-SH}, L--SV by the four boundary conditions \cref{eq:20.3-b} and the flux fractions \cref{eq:20.3-flux} $\to$ flux-weighted angle and branch average \cref{eq:20.3-avg} $\to$ at equal speeds on both sides no refraction, no critical angle and no conversion, so $\bar t_{12} \to t_{12}(0)$. Unitarity of the scattering matrix at fixed $(\omega, Q_\parallel)$ gives the reciprocity \cref{eq:20.3-recip}; diffuse limit \cref{eq:20.3-dmm}. $\mathfrak{T}$: harmonic continuum elasticity, wavelength between the lattice spacing and the film thickness.
& Within \SI{10}{\percent} in both directions for $c_{\mathrm{LA}}(\mathrm{film})/c_{\mathrm{LA}}(\mathrm{Si}) = 1.146$--1.242 in the aluminum-like family (density \SI{2.70}{g.cm^{-3}}, $c_{\mathrm{T}}/c_{\mathrm{LA}} = 0.498$ of aluminum at zero temperature), where $\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{film}} \approx \mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{Si}}$. Aluminum on silicon (ratio 0.737): $t_{12}(0) = 0.994$ against $\bar t_{\mathrm{Si\to Al}} = 0.938$ and $\bar t_{\mathrm{Al\to Si}} = 0.368$; reciprocity residual $2\times10^{-15}$. Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-amm}): $x = c_{\mathrm{LA}}(\mathrm{film})/c_{\mathrm{LA}}(\mathrm{Si})$, 0.35--2; $y$ = transmission; impedance law, both averages, leading correction, diffuse mismatch; shaded 1.146--1.242.
& \Fsize{factor $\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{Si}}/\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{Al}} = 0.392$ on the escape from aluminum; the corrected form within \SI{6.9}{\percent} of the average over the plotted range}. Leading correction: total reflection of the slower medium's phonons beyond the critical angles, written as reciprocity applied to the impedance law on the slower side, $t_{12}(0)\min(1, \mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{out}}/\mathfrak{n}_{\mathrm{in}})$ (0.390 against 0.368 for the escape from aluminum). Not included: silicon's anisotropy ($\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 0.327$--0.405 between its $[100]$ and $[111]$ speeds), interlayers (normal-incidence minimum 0.844 for amorphous SiO$_2$), partial diffuseness; room-temperature aluminum constants ($\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 0.327$).
& \cref{sec:20.3} \\

Lumped film absorption (no box), \cref{eq:20.3-pabs,eq:20.3-tl}
& \cite[Eq.~(17)]{kelsey2023g4cmp}
& Published form: escape unabsorbed with probability $\exp(-4d_{\mathrm{f}}/\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{f}})$ after one round trip. Chain: round trips at fixed angle \cref{eq:20.3-pabs-theta} $\to$ direction randomized at every pass, survival $2E_3(2d_{\mathrm{f}}/\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{f}})$, escape $\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}}$, \cref{eq:20.3-pabs} $\to$ $\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 1$ and the angle average moved into the exponent ($\langle1/\cos\theta\rangle = 2$) gives the published form. Escape time \cref{eq:20.3-tl} from the same counting in time. $\mathfrak{T}$: kinetic (rate) description of the film phonons, frequency-independent interface.
& At $\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 1$: within \SI{10}{\percent} of \cref{eq:20.3-pabs} for $d_{\mathrm{f}}/\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{f}} < 0.074$ and above 0.55, above it everywhere (at most \SI{14}{\percent}, at $d_{\mathrm{f}}/\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{f}} = 0.24$). Film randomized in direction and branch faster than it escapes; wavelengths below $d_{\mathrm{f}}$; no loss at the top face.
& \Fsize{factor $1/\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 2.72$ in the thin-film limit for aluminum on silicon, 1.82 for \SI{60}{nm} at $\mathfrak{l}_{\mathrm{f}} = v_st_0$}. The escape probability: the published form returns every unabsorbed phonon after one round trip, while $\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}} = 0.368$ (acoustic) or 0.282 (diffuse) makes it return $1/\bar t_{\mathrm{esc}}$ times; $t_l = 0.187$--\SI{0.244}{ns} for \SI{60}{nm} (0.223--\SI{0.281}{ns} with room-temperature aluminum constants). Without in-film mixing, the \SI{56}{\percent} of the flux beyond the transverse critical angle never escapes.
& \cref{sec:20.3} \\

Pair-breaking efficiency (\cref{lim:20.4a}), \cref{eq:20.4-below}; \cref{eq:20.4-split,eq:20.4-tpb,eq:20.4-emit,eq:20.4-ppb,eq:20.4-cascade}
& \cite[pp.~2, 5, Fig.~4]{guruswamy2014quasiparticle}; \cite{kaplan1976quasiparticle}
& Published form: $\eta_s = 2\Delta/h\nu_s$ for $2\Delta \le h\nu_s \le 4\Delta$, independent of the phonon loss time; $\eta_s = 0.57$--0.6 in infinite volume. Chain: golden rule with the case-I factors \cref{eq:7.2-cohE} at $v_{\mathrm{F}}q \gg E_{\mathrm{ath}}$ $\to$ split \cref{eq:20.4-split}, pair-breaking time \cref{eq:20.4-tpb} (normal width times $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{I}}$, width $\propto E_{\mathrm{ath}}$ for $\alpha^2F = b_\alpha E_{\mathrm{ath}}^2$), emission density \cref{eq:20.4-emit} $\to$ a pair-breaking emission needs $E \ge 3\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ $\to$ $N_{\mathrm{qp}} = 2$ below $4\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$, \cref{eq:20.4-below} $\to$ above: lumped film with exponential escape after $t_l$, \cref{eq:20.4-ppb} $\to$ mean-number recursion \cref{eq:20.4-cascade} (dilute quasiparticles, $T = 0$, no recombination during the cascade). $\mathfrak{T}$: detector stage (kinetics, first order in each rate).
& Exact below $4\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ for $T \ll T_c$, any film and any $\alpha^2F$. Above: lumped film, $T = 0$, $\alpha^2F \propto E_{\mathrm{ath}}^2$ (aluminum up to about \SI{10}{meV}); phonon and photon seeds differ by at most 0.007 (at $5\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$). Plot (\cref{fig:20-limit-qp}): $x = E_{\mathrm{ath}}/\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$, 2--10; $y = Y_{\mathrm{qp}}$; $2\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}/E_{\mathrm{ath}}$ dashed; cascade (photon seed) at $t_l/t_0 = 0.5$, 1, 2, 4, 8, $\infty$; kinetic-equation points read off Fig.~4 of the source (digitized to $\pm0.005$); shaded $E_{\mathrm{ath}} < 4\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$.
& \Fsize{$\le 0.011$ for $t_l/t_0 \le 4$; 0.036--0.041 at $t_l/t_0 = 8$ (kinetic equations above the cascade, 5--$10\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$)}. The terms of the Chang--Scalapino equations that the cascade drops: finite temperature ($T/T_c = 0.1$), measured $\alpha^2F$, recombination and reabsorption of $2\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ phonons; the readout probe adds about \SI{2}{\percent}, and counting the excess energy of the quasiparticles about \SI{4}{\percent}. Above $4\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ escape dominates: over the arrival band 13.6--$43.8\,\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ the yield falls by \SI{21.5}{\percent} at $t_l/t_0 = 0.5$ and by \SI{1.9}{\percent} at 8, and lies 47--\SI{58}{\percent} below the no-escape value at $t_l/t_0 = 0.5$.
& \cref{sec:20.4} \\

Collection ratio of Luke phonons, one film type (no box), \cref{eq:20.5-sign}; mean-field kernels \cref{eq:20.5-Gabs,eq:20.5-meanfield}
& ---
& Published form: none. Linear transport $\to$ collection kernels \cref{eq:20.5-kernel} and the functionals \cref{eq:20.5-functionals} $\to$ mean-field limit (diffusion across the crystal faster than absorption, diffuse grey faces): position-independent absorption rate \cref{eq:20.5-Gabs}, triangular solve of \cref{eq:20.5-meanfield} for the time-integrated spectrum $\to$ one film type, primaries absorbed with their mean yield $\bar Y_{\mathrm{prim}}$, absorbed fraction common to all phonons above $2\Delta_{\mathrm{BCS}}$ $\to$ $\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} - 1 = s_{\mathrm{ng}} - (1 - \kappa_{\mathrm{sub}})s_{\mathrm{sub}}$. $\mathfrak{T}$: incoherent propagation, $n_A = 2$ for three-phonon decay; lumped films with the Kaplan-cascade yield of \cref{sec:20.4} and film escape at the $t_l/t_0$ of \cref{sec:20.3}; every phonon a film emits after its first pair break is returned to the crystal (energy conserved).
& Diffusion time across the crystal short against the absorption time (\SI{15}{\micro s} for a \SI{4}{mm} silicon slab with \SI{10}{\percent} film coverage); faces grey and diffuse; one film type; absorption per bounce independent of energy above threshold (exact for thick films, within 0.011 with escape). No Limit Check; \cref{fig:20-rcoll} shows the estimate: energy conserved, one or two films, \SI{60}{nm} to thick, 0.81--1.02 over 0.75--\SI{4.7}{meV} and 0.82--1.01 at \SI{0.84}{meV}; escape moves one film by $< 0.002$ at a bare-face loss of $10^{-3}$ (numerical, not exact); a uniform sink of 0.3 per hit keeps it $\le 1.03$; the lumped film's selective sink gives an upper envelope 1.05--1.10 (\SI{300}{nm}) and 1.30--1.53 (\SI{60}{nm}); 1.128 is not reached by the transport estimate, and not excluded until computed kernels test real detectors (\cref{app:E.25}); the two-bias measurement of \cref{sec:19.5} decides.
& \Fsize{0.008 from the extra bare-face loss of a two-film detector; $10^{-3}$ for a thick film}. A second film type that absorbs only part of the Luke spectrum lengthens its dwell time ($\mathfrak{r}_{\mathrm{coll}} = 0.992$ with sub-gap phonons absorbed) and adds its own near-gap yield (up to $+0.09$ when film phonons are deleted; with them returned the ratio stays $\le 1.02$); the fate of phonons that leave a film (returned here; a selective sink would raise the ratio to the envelope above); position dependence of the kernels (position spread) beyond the mean field; near-gap transparency of thin films.
& \cref{sec:20.5} \\
\midrule
High-voltage readout ceiling (no box), \cref{eq:20.5-hv}
& ---
& Published form: none; the phonon energy of a biased detector is $E_{\mathrm{ph}} = E_R + N_{\mathrm{eh}}eV_{\mathrm{bias}}$ with $\langle N_{\mathrm{eh}}\rangle = Y_qE_R/\SI{3.8}{eV}$~\cite[Eq.~(1)]{albakry2023first}. Chain: equal total phonon energy fixes the electron-recoil energy $E_R(1 + gY'_q)/(1 + g)$ $\to$ difference of mean detected pairs $f_{\mathrm{det}}(E_R/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{eh}})(1 - Y'_q)/(1 + g)$ $\to$ the Luke share at fixed $E_{\mathrm{ph}}$ is linear in $N_{\mathrm{det}}$ $\to$ variances $F_{\mathrm{eff}}\langle N_{\mathrm{det}}\rangle$ with the cascade and partial-charge terms of \cref{eq:20.1-Fpos} bracketed by the binomial and mean-charge limits $\to$ separation \cref{eq:20.5-sep}. $\mathfrak{T}$: Gaussian separation measure; primary phonon energy equal to the recoil energy (\cref{lim:19.5a}).
& Ideal readout of the Luke share (contrast one, no baseline noise, no position spread); event position spread of the collection neglected (the position term of \cref{eq:20.1-Fpos}, $1.2\times10^{-4}\bar N_{\mathrm{eh}}$ at \SI{95}{\percent} collection, is below \SI{1}{\percent} of the Fano factor at ten pairs); measured $Y_q$, $F_{\mathrm{NR}}$ at 0.1--\SI{0.89}{keV}. No Limit Check; \cref{fig:20-ceiling} shows it against $E_R$.
& \Fsize{factor 1.23 between the binomial and mean-charge limits at \SI{220}{eV} (1.22 against 1.50)}. The spread of induced charge per pair, between all-or-nothing and equal partial charges; baseline noise and position spread lower the ceiling further; the yield and Fano factor below the binary threshold are measured, not derived (\cref{sec:19.2}).
& \cref{sec:20.5} \\
\midrule
Mean inverse speed of the standard halo (\cref{lim:21.1a}), \cref{eq:D.1-eta1,eq:D.1-eta2}
& \cite[Eqs.~(B.4), (B.5)]{essig2016direct}
& Published form: the same two branches at a fixed Earth speed, with $v_0 = 230$, $v_{\mathrm{esc}} = 600$ and $v_{\mathrm{E}} = \SI{240}{km.s^{-1}}$. Chain: halo functional \cref{eq:21.1-functional} with an angle-averaged response $\to$ angular average of the energy delta function, $\Theta(v - \vmin)/2qv$, and integration by parts, \cref{eq:21.1-etaform}, with $\eta$ of \cref{eq:1.3-eta} $\to$ laboratory distribution \cref{eq:1.2-flab} of the truncated Maxwellian \cref{eq:1.2-fgal} $\to$ the integrals of \cref{app:D.1} $\to$ the exposure average of $\eta$ over $|\vect{v}_{\mathrm{E}}(t)|$ (\cref{app:A.8}) replaced by its value at $v_{\mathrm{E}}^{\mathrm{ref}} = \SI{250.5}{km.s^{-1}}$. $\mathfrak{T}$: not applicable (halo side only).
& Exact for the standard halo at one Earth speed. As a stand-in for the yearly average: the average exceeds it by less than \SI{1}{\percent} for $\vmin \le \SI{430.3}{km.s^{-1}}$ and by less than \SI{10}{\percent} for $\vmin \le \SI{735.6}{km.s^{-1}}$. Plot (\cref{fig:21-limit-eta}): $x = \vmin$, 0--\SI{812}{km.s^{-1}}; top $y = \eta$ in units of $1/c$, closed form at $v_{\mathrm{E}}^{\mathrm{ref}}$ (solid), yearly average with the exact $|\vect{v}_{\mathrm{E}}(t)|$ on 7305 days (dashed), no escape cut (dotted); bottom, ratio of the yearly average to the closed form and the second-order correction; shaded $\vmin \le \SI{735.6}{km.s^{-1}}$.
& \Fsize{$+\SI{1}{\percent}$ at \SI{430}{km.s^{-1}}, $+\SI{10}{\percent}$ at \SI{736}{km.s^{-1}}, $+\SI{20}{\percent}$ at \SI{760}{km.s^{-1}}}. The second-order term in $\delta v_{\mathrm{E}} = |\vect{v}_{\mathrm{E}}(t)| - v_{\mathrm{E}}^{\mathrm{ref}}$, \cref{eq:21.1-annualcorr}, with $\langle\delta v_{\mathrm{E}}\rangle = \SI{1.61}{km.s^{-1}}$ and $\langle\delta v_{\mathrm{E}}^2\rangle = \SI{108.3}{km^2.s^{-2}}$; within \SI{1}{\percent} of the yearly average up to \SI{786.3}{km.s^{-1}}. Above $v_{\mathrm{max}} = \SI{794.5}{km.s^{-1}}$ the closed form vanishes while the yearly average stays positive up to \SI{810.4}{km.s^{-1}}.
& \cref{sec:21.1} \\
\midrule
Halo-independent bound for a speed-only response (\cref{lim:21.2a}), \cref{eq:21.2-hib}; \cref{eq:21.2-column,eq:21.2-multipole}
& \cite[Eqs.~(A1)--(A2)]{fox2011integrating}; \cite[Sect.~2]{chen2021halo}; \cite[Eqs.~(2.17), (2.18)]{bernreuther2024extracting}
& Published form: a limit on $\rho_\chi\sigma g_1/\mchi$ for the step halo $g = g_1\Theta(v_1 - v)$, one bin; for electrons, counts linear in steps of $\tilde\eta$. Chain: halo functional \cref{eq:21.1-exposure} $\to$ counts $\int\mathsf{k}_i\,\dd\Xi$ with the stream kernel \cref{eq:21.2-stream} $\to$ semi-infinite program \cref{eq:C.12-sip} for $\tilde\eta(u_0)$, \cref{eq:21.2-etatilde} $\to$ angle-averaged response, fixed $\vect{v}_{\mathrm{E}}$: $\mathsf{k}_i = \mathcal{A}_i(v)/v$ with the nondecreasing step column \cref{eq:21.2-column} $\to$ one stream at $u_0$ and the one-bin certificate $\lambda_j = 1/\mathcal{A}_j(u_0)$ give \cref{eq:21.2-hib} exactly (\cref{app:D.46}). Directional: circle average \cref{eq:D.46-circle} $\to$ \cref{eq:21.2-multipole}, sidereal mean over declination, galactic support. $\mathfrak{T}$: the label of the response tables that give $\mathsf{k}_i$; the bound itself adds none.
& Exact for a response independent of $\hat{\vect{q}}$ with nonnegative bin weights and constant $\vect{v}_{\mathrm{E}}$. Silicon at \SI{2}{MeV}, one- and two-pair bins of the cascade of \cref{sec:18.3}, crystal at \ang{45}\,N with $[001]$ up, integrated over sidereal time, model anisotropy of \cref{sec:11.7}: within \SI{10}{\percent} of the linear program for every $u_0$ on a \SI{5}{km.s^{-1}} grid from 340 to \SI{790}{km.s^{-1}} except 340, 345, 365 and \SI{470}{km.s^{-1}}; within \SI{2.0}{\percent} for $u_0 \ge \SI{560}{km.s^{-1}}$. Plot (\cref{fig:21-limit-hib}): $x = u_0$, 340--\SI{800}{km.s^{-1}}; $y$ = program over formula; formula (1), program (dots), first-order correction (dashed); shaded where within \SI{10}{\percent}.
& \Fsize{$+$2 to $+$\SI{15}{\percent} below \SI{560}{km.s^{-1}} (sampled from \SI{340}{km.s^{-1}}), at most $+\SI{2.0}{\percent}$ above; $+\SI{0.4}{\percent}$ at \SI{790}{km.s^{-1}}}. First order in the anisotropy: the smallest sidereal-mean kernel of the tightest bin among the declinations allowed at $u_0$, which reproduces the program to \SI{1.8}{\percent}. Largest near the onset speed of a bin, where every reachable pair has $\vmin \approx u_0$ and carries the full anisotropy; a search binned in sidereal time keeps the crystal-frame anisotropy (kernel down to 0.33 of its average in the model).
& \cref{sec:21.2} \\
